# Klein 유기화학 — Chapters 1–6: summaries + literature-based problems

## Chapter 1 — 일반화학 복습하기 (A Review of General Chemistry)  (PDF p1–8, book p58–65)

Note: the PDF begins mid-summary (book p58, from §1.6 onward); the §1.1–1.5 summary text is not in these pages.

### Key concepts (summary, §1.6–1.13 + skill builders 1.1–1.10)
- §1.6 Atomic orbitals, nodes (ψ = 0); electron density; filling orbitals: Aufbau, Pauli exclusion, Hund's rule.
- §1.7 Valence bond theory: bonding = constructive overlap of atomic orbitals; σ bonds have density on the bond axis.
- §1.8 MO theory / LCAO: bonding MO (constructive), antibonding MO (destructive); MOs belong to the whole molecule; HOMO and LUMO.
- §1.9 Hybridization: sp3 (methane, tetrahedral), sp2 (ethylene, trigonal planar, σ + π), sp (acetylene, linear, σ + 2π). Triple bond shorter and stronger than double, which is shorter and stronger than single.
- §1.10 VSEPR geometry; steric number = σ bonds + lone pairs → tetrahedral, trigonal pyramidal, bent, trigonal planar, linear.
- §1.11 Dipole moment (μ, debye); % ionic character; molecular dipole = vector sum of bond dipoles.
- §1.12 Intermolecular forces: dipole–dipole, hydrogen bonding (higher bp), London dispersion (larger surface area → stronger; branching lowers bp).
- §1.13 Solubility: "like dissolves like"; soaps are amphiphilic (hydrophilic head, hydrophobic tail), forming micelles.
- Skill builders: constitutional isomers; Lewis dot structures; formal charge; partial charges (δ+/δ−); electron configurations; hybridization from bond types (4 single = sp3, 1 double = sp2, triple = sp); geometry via steric number; net molecular dipole; predicting physical properties (dipole–dipole, H-bonding, carbon count/branching).

#### 1.68 (종합 문제; book p63, PDF p6) — footnote 1 → Tetrahedron Lett. 2012, 53, 4536–4537
- Compounds: tert-butyl isocyanide **A**, (CH3)3C–N⁺≡C⁻ → tert-butyl isocyanide dichloride (isonitrile dihalide / carbonimidic dichloride) **B**, (CH3)3C–N=CCl2. Highlighted: the N and terminal C of A; the N and C of B. Context: converting an isonitrile to its dihalide temporarily "masks" isonitrile reactivity.
- SMILES: A `CC(C)(C)[N+]#[C-]` [high]; B `CC(C)(C)N=C(Cl)Cl` [high]. No reagents shown (the conversion uses Cl2, which is not drawn).
- Questions: (a) hybridization of the highlighted atoms in A; (b) which atomic orbital holds the carbon lone pair in A; (c) the C–N–C angle in A; (d) hybridization of the highlighted atoms in B; (e) which orbital holds the N lone pair in B; (f) the C–N–C angle in B.
- Expected answers: (a) N sp, C sp; (b) an sp orbital; (c) ~180°; (d) N sp2, C sp2; (e) an sp2 orbital; (f) ~120° (somewhat less because of the lone pair). [high]
- Concept: hybridization and geometry; lone-pair orbital type.

#### 1.69 (book p63, PDF p6) — footnote 2 → Org. Lett. 1999, 1, 1123–1125
- Context: branching / steric hindrance ("입체 장애"); more hindered C=C π bonds react more slowly.
- Molecule drawn: (CH3)2C=CH–CH2–CH2–CH(CH3)–CH=CH2, i.e. 3,7-dimethylocta-1,6-diene (dihydromyrcene). SMILES `CC(C)=CCCC(C)C=C` [med-high, read from the expanded drawing].
- Question: identify each π bond and say which is more sterically hindered. Note: hindrance is not the same as steric number.
- Answer: the trisubstituted C6=C7 (with the two methyls) is more hindered; the terminal monosubstituted CH=CH2 is less hindered (so selective reagents attack the terminal alkene). [high]

#### 1.70 (book p63) — no footnote
Bond-length table, C–X in Å:

| | X = F | X = Cl | X = Br | X = I |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| sp3 C | 1.40 | 1.79 | 1.97 | 2.16 |
| sp2 C | 1.34 | — | 1.88 | 2.10 |
| sp C | 1.27 | 1.63 | 1.79 | — |

It asks whether C(sp2)–Cl or C(sp)–I is shorter. It is listed here only for completeness because it has no literature footnote.

#### 1.71 (book p63, PDF p6) — footnote 3 → Tetrahedron Lett. 2003, 44, 9165–9167
- PET tracer [18F]fluorocholine synthesis: 18F–CH2CH2–Br + Me2N–CH2CH2–OH (2-(dimethylamino)ethanol) → [18F]F–CH2CH2–N⁺(CH3)2–CH2CH2–OH Br⁻ (N-(2-[18F]fluoroethyl)-N,N-dimethylethanolammonium bromide, i.e. [18F]fluoroethylcholine). The drawing shows an ethyl linker on the 18F side.
- SMILES: `[18F]CCBr`, `CN(C)CCO`, `C[N+](C)(CC[18F])CCO.[Br-]` [high].
- Questions: (a) hybridization of every atom other than C, H and O in the three compounds (lone pairs are not drawn); (b) the C–N–C angles in the product.
- Answers: F sp3; Br sp3 (in the alkyl bromide); amine N sp3; ammonium N sp3 (Br⁻ treated as having no hybridization, or sp3 by convention); all C–N–C angles ~109.5°. [high]

#### 1.72 (도전 문제; book p64, PDF p7) — footnote 4 → Tetrahedron 2006, 62, 10968–10979
- Epichlorohydrin (**1**, 2-(chloromethyl)oxirane, `ClCC1CO1`) + phenol (**2**, `Oc1ccccc1`), NaOH, heat → **3** 1-chloro-3-phenoxypropan-2-ol `OC(COc1ccccc1)CCl` + **4** phenyl glycidyl ether `C(Oc1ccccc1)C1CO1` [high].
- Observation: at room temperature the two liquid products are hard to separate, but on heating they separate easily. Explain.
- Expected answer: 3 has an O–H and forms intermolecular hydrogen bonds; 4 has none. 3 therefore has a much higher boiling point, so heating (distillation) separates 4 (more volatile) from 3. [med-high] Concept: hydrogen bonding and boiling point.

#### 1.73 (book p64, PDF p7) — footnote 5 → Org. Lett. 1999, 1, 1713–1715
- Phenalamide A2 (antibiotic, antifungal, antiviral natural product): a boronate ester used in the first total synthesis. Structure: an allylic pinacol boronate, CH2=CH–CH(Bpin)–(4H-1,3-dioxin-4-yl). The ring is a six-membered O–CH2–O–CH=CH–CH ring.
- SMILES `C=CC(B1OC(C)(C)C(C)(C)O1)C1C=COCO1` [med; ring connectivity read from the drawing].
- Questions: (a) boron hybridization; (b) predict the O–B–O angle and suggest why the real angle deviates; (c) draw all lone pairs (hint: no formal charges).
- Answers: (a) sp2; (b) predicted 120°, actual smaller (~110°) because B is in a five-membered dioxaborolane ring (pentagon interior ~108°), so ring strain/geometry constrains it; (c) two lone pairs on each O (4 O = 8 lone pairs); B has none (empty p orbital). [high]

#### 1.74 (book p64, PDF p7) — footnote 6 → J. Org. Chem. 2009, 74, 1099–1113
- Oxazolidinone synthesis: hydroximoyl chloride **1**, benzohydroximoyl chloride PhC(Cl)=N–OH `ON=C(Cl)c1ccccc1`, → nitrile oxide **2**, benzonitrile oxide PhC≡N⁺–O⁻ `[O-][N+]#Cc1ccccc1` (drawn without charges as C≡N–Ö:) [high].
- Questions (book p64 right column): (a) the missing formal charges in 1 and 2; (b) which is more soluble in a polar solvent, and why; (c) how much the C–C–N angle increases from 1 to 2.
- Answers: (a) 1 has none; in 2 N is +1 and O is −1; (b) 2 is more soluble (charge-separated, large dipole; 1 can also H-bond, so this is debatable; textbook answer likely 2) [med]; (c) 120° → 180°, an increase of ~60° [high].

#### 1.75 (book p64, PDF p7) — footnote 7 → Tetrahedron Lett. 2001, 42, 8579–8582
- Estradiol derivative (breast-cancer treatment candidate). Estradiol skeleton (aromatic A ring with 3-OH, 18-CH3), with C17 bearing OH and a C≡C–CH=CH–CH=C=CH2 chain (17α-alkynyl enyne–allene; Ca = terminal CH2, Cb = central allene C, Cc = allene CH).
- SMILES (no stereo shown) `CC12CCC3c4ccc(O)cc4CCC3C1CCC2(O)C#CC=CC=C=C` [med-high].
- Questions: (a) hybridization of Ca, Cb, Cc; (b) the H–Ca–Cb angle; (c) the Ca–Cb–Cc angle; (d) Cb has two π bonds; draw the relative orientation of its two p orbitals and the p orbitals of Ca and Cc.
- Answers: (a) Ca sp2, Cb sp, Cc sp2; (b) ~120°; (c) 180°; (d) Cb's two p orbitals are perpendicular, so the Ca and Cc p orbitals are perpendicular to each other (the allene's terminal substituent planes are orthogonal). [high]

#### 1.76 (book p64, PDF p7) — footnote 8 → Org. Lett. 2001, 3, 3843–3846
- Enzyme-mimic / foldamer study compound: Boc-Leu-NH-iPr, (CH3)3C–O–C(=O)–NH–CH(CH2CH(CH3)2)–C(=O)–NH–CH(CH3)2. Highlighted: the carbamate C=O oxygen (Boc) and the N(–H) of the isopropylamide.
- SMILES `CC(C)CC(NC(=O)OC(C)(C)C)C(=O)NC(C)C` [high; no stereo drawn].
- Questions: (a) "the compound has two N–C–N units with different bond angles"; predict the difference and explain (as printed; the wording looks garbled, likely meaning the two amide/carbamate N environments or the C–N–C angles, so the intended comparison is uncertain); (b) the highlighted regions prefer to sit close in 3D; explain the interaction and draw it.
- Answer (b): an intramolecular hydrogen bond, N–H (isopropylamide) ··· O=C (Boc carbamate), closing a 7-membered ring (γ-turn/C7-type). [high]

#### 1.77 (book p65, PDF p8) — footnote 9 → J. Am. Chem. Soc. 2009, 131, 8833–8838
- Molecularly imprinted polymers. Guests: bisphenol A **1** `CC(C)(c1ccc(O)cc1)c1ccc(O)cc1` and 4,4'-methylenedianiline **2** `Nc1ccc(Cc2ccc(N)cc2)cc1` (drawn with a CH2 linker; [med] that the linker is CH2). Polymer A binding site: two benzoic-acid (–COOH) groups facing each other. Polymer B binding site: two pyridine N's.
- Facts: A binds 1 and 2 about equally (limited selectivity); B binds 1 strongly and 2 weakly.
- Task: draw how 1 and 2 fit each cavity and explain the selectivity.
- Answer: COOH is both an H-bond donor and acceptor, so it H-bonds with Ar–OH and Ar–NH2 alike (no discrimination). Pyridine N is only an acceptor and pairs best with the stronger, more acidic O–H donor of the phenol; aniline N–H is a weaker donor, so 2 binds weakly. [med-high]

## Chapter 2 — 분자 표현법 (Molecular Representations)  (PDF p9–16, book p101–108)

### Key concepts (개념과 용어의 복습)
- §2.1 Lewis, partially condensed and condensed structures; a molecular formula gives no structural information.
- §2.2 Bond-line structures (C and most H not drawn).
- §2.3 Functional groups determine chemistry.
- §2.4 Formal charge; carbon with a charge has 3 bonds.
- §2.5 Lone pairs are usually omitted in bond-line structures and must be inferred.
- §2.6 Wedges/dashes for 3D; Fischer and Haworth projections.
- §2.7–2.13 Resonance: structures linked by double-headed arrows in brackets; resonance stabilization = delocalization of charge. Curved-arrow rules: don't break single bonds; never exceed an octet for second-row elements. Show all formal charges. Five patterns: (1) allylic lone pair, (2) allylic carbocation, (3) lone pair adjacent to C⁺, (4) π bond between atoms of different electronegativity, (5) conjugated π bonds in a ring. Significance rules: max full octets > fewest formal charges > negative charge on the more electronegative atom; equivalent structures contribute equally. Resonance hybrid shown with partial bonds/charges. Delocalized lone pairs occupy p orbitals (atom becomes sp2); localized ones don't; an atom with both a π bond and a lone pair doesn't delocalize both.
- Skill builders 2.1–2.10 cover each of these.

#### 2.68 (종합 문제; book p106, PDF p14) — footnote 2 → Org. Lett. 2015, 17, 1529–1532
- (+)-Dragmacidin D (marine sponge alkaloid; antiviral, antifungal, antibacterial). Structure: 2-aminoimidazolium, an imidazole ring with C2=N⁺H2, two ring N–H, and C4–R. R is a CH(CH3) (wavy/hashed stereocenter) attached at C4 of a 7-hydroxyindol-3-yl unit, linked at indole C3 to a pyrazin-2(1H)-one, whose other carbon bears a 6-bromoindol-3-yl.
- SMILES (stereo omitted) `CC(c1c[nH]c(=[NH2+])[nH]1)c1ccc(O)c2[nH]cc(-c3cnc(-c4c[nH]c5cc(Br)ccc45)c(=O)[nH]3)c12` [med; pyrazinone regiochemistry read from the drawing].
- Question: the cation is delocalized; draw all resonance structures delocalizing the charge, then the hybrid.
- Answer: three equivalent-type guanidinium-like structures with the + on each of the three N's (exocyclic NH2 and the two ring NH); the hybrid has a partial positive charge (δ+) on all three N's and partial C=N character. [high]

#### 2.69 (book p106, PDF p14) — footnote 3 → Cryst. Growth Des. 2012, 12, 4311–4314
- CL-20 (hexanitrohexaazaisowurtzitane) and HMX (octahydro-1,3,5,7-tetranitro-1,3,5,7-tetrazocine). A 2:1 CL-20:HMX cocrystal is more powerful than HMX with similar sensitivity.
- SMILES: HMX `C1N(CN(CN(CN1[N+](=O)[O-])[N+](=O)[O-])[N+](=O)[O-])[N+](=O)[O-]`; CL-20 cage `O=[N+]([O-])N1C2C3N(C4C(N3[N+](=O)[O-])N(C1C(N2[N+](=O)[O-])N4[N+](=O)[O-])[N+](=O)[O-])[N+](=O)[O-]` [med for the CL-20 SMILES; well-known structure].
- Questions: (a) molecular formulas; (b) is the lone pair on a ring N localized or delocalized?
- Answers: (a) CL-20 = C6H6N12O12; HMX = C4H8N8O8; (b) delocalized into the N=O of the nitro group (nitramine N–NO2 resonance), so the ring N is sp2/trigonal planar. [high]

#### 2.70 (book p106, PDF p14) — footnote 4 → J. Am. Chem. Soc. 1971, 93, 4332–4334
- W. S. Johnson biomimetic progesterone synthesis (polyene cyclization). Intermediate shown: a tetracyclic D-homo/A-ring cyclopentene steroid skeleton (cyclopentene A ring with a methyl on the double bond, angular methyls, trans ring fusions with H's dashed/wedged), where C17 bears an exocyclic C=C(CH3)–O–C⁺ ring: a 1,3-dioxolan-2-ylium cation (an enol ether attached to a dioxolanyl cation).
- Question: use resonance to explain why this intermediate is especially stable.
- Answer: the carbocation C2 of the dioxolane is flanked by two O atoms, each with a lone pair that delocalizes the + charge (oxocarbenium resonance). Including the third, vinyl-ether O, the + is shared over three O's (orthoester-derived trialkoxycarbenium-type cation); all atoms keep octets. [high]
- SMILES: uncertain (full tetracyclic skeleton stereochemistry); cation fragment `C(C)(=C<steroid>)O[C+]1OCCO1`.

#### 2.71 (book p107, PDF p15) — footnote 5 → Angew. Chem. Int. Ed. 2005, 44, 1012–1044
- Misassigned natural-product structures (structure revision). (a) Cholesterol: the originally proposed (wrong) structure, a strange polycyclic with an OH, a cyclopentene and the same C8 side chain, vs. the correct one, `C[C@H](CCCC(C)C)[C@H]1CC[C@H]2[C@@H]3CC=C4C[C@@H](O)CC[C@]4(C)[C@H]3CC[C@]12C`. (b) Porritoxin: proposed = benzoxazocine lactam (8-membered ring with C(=O)NH–CH2CH2–O–CH2); correct = isoindolinone N-(2-hydroxyethyl), with a prenyloxy group, aromatic CH3 and OCH3.
- Correct porritoxin SMILES `CC(C)=CCOc1cc2C(=O)N(CCO)Cc2c(OC)c1C` [med].
- Task: identify all functional groups in each pair; compare similarities and differences.
- Answer: (a) both have a secondary alcohol, alkene and alkyl side chain; the proposed structure has a different ring system and no C5=C6 trisubstituted alkene in the same place. (b) Both have an ether (prenyl aryl ether, an alkene), an aryl methyl ether and an amide; the proposed one is a cyclic ether + secondary lactam (8-membered), the correct one a tertiary γ-lactam + primary alcohol (same formula C17H23NO4? [low]).

#### 2.72 (book p107, PDF p15) — footnote 6 → Chem. Rev. 1993, 93, 381–433
- Triarylmethane dyes: generic Ar2C=C6H4=N⁺R2 Cl⁻ with one ring bearing NR2, one para-X. Basic green 4 (malachite green: X = H, R = CH3); Basic red 9 (pararosaniline: X = NH2, R = H); Basic violet 4 (ethyl violet: X = N(C2H5)2, R = C2H5).
- Malachite green SMILES `CN(C)c1ccc(cc1)C(=C1C=CC(C=C1)=[N+](C)C)c1ccccc1.[Cl-]` [high].
- Questions: (a) resonance structures showing delocalization of the + charge for basic green 4; (b) which has greater resonance stabilization, basic green 4 or basic violet 4?
- Answers: (a) + on each of the two N's and on the central C, plus ring ortho/para carbons; (b) basic violet 4: its third ring carries an NEt2 lone pair that can delocalize the charge over 3 N atoms. [high]

#### 2.73 (book p107, PDF p15; IR problem) — footnote 7 → J. Chem. Ed. 2006, 83, 1534–1536
- Vinyl acetate **1** (`C=COC(C)=O`) → poly(vinyl acetate) **2**; partial hydrolysis → **3** (some OAc remaining); NaOH/H2O full hydrolysis → poly(vinyl alcohol) **4**.
- Question: how to use IR to confirm 3 → 4 (complete hydrolysis of acetate).
- Answer: the ester C=O stretch (~1740 cm⁻¹) and C–O ester stretches (~1240 cm⁻¹) disappear completely; the broad O–H (~3200–3500 cm⁻¹) remains/grows. [high]

#### 2.74 (book p107, PDF p15; IR problem) — footnote 8 → J. Chem. Ed. 2006, 83, 287–289
- Knoevenagel/lactonization: 4-(diethylamino)salicylaldehyde **1** `CCN(CC)c1ccc(C=O)c(O)c1` + ethyl acetoacetate `CCOC(=O)CC(C)=O`, piperidine → 3-acetyl-7-(diethylamino)coumarin **2** `CCN(CC)c1ccc2cc(C(C)=O)c(=O)oc2c1` [high].
- Question: give at least three IR observations that confirm 1 → 2.
- Answer: phenol O–H broad band disappears; aldehyde C–H stretches (~2720/2820 cm⁻¹) disappear; aldehyde C=O (~1660–1700, conjugated/H-bonded) replaced by lactone C=O (~1720–1740) plus ketone C=O (~1680); the ester ethyl C–H/C–O pattern of acetoacetate is absent. [high]

#### 2.75 (도전 문제; book p107, PDF p15) — footnote 9 → Soft Matter 2012, 8, 5486–5492
- Gelator intermediate: N-(3,4,5-tris(dodecyloxy)benzyl)-4-formylbenzamide. SMILES `CCCCCCCCCCCCOc1cc(CNC(=O)c2ccc(C=O)cc2)cc(OCCCCCCCCCCCC)c1OCCCCCCCCCCCC` [high].
- Questions: (a) identify the functional groups; (b) of the two C–N bonds, one rotates freely and one is restricted; identify the restricted one and explain with resonance.
- Answers: (a) three ethers (aryl alkyl), a secondary amide, an aldehyde, two aromatic rings; (b) the amide C(=O)–N bond has partial double-bond character (N lone pair delocalized onto O); the benzylic CH2–N bond rotates freely. [high]

#### 2.76 (book p108, PDF p16) — footnote 10 → Langmuir 2012, 28, 14567–14572
- Imine formation RCHO + R'NH2 → RCH=NR'. Reactants: **A** = 3,5-bis(10-(pyridinium-1-yl)decyloxy)benzaldehyde (dication); **B** = 1,5-diaminopentane (cadaverine) `NCCCCCN`; **C** = p-xylylenediamine `NCc1ccc(CN)cc1`; **D** = 3,4-bis(10-pyridiniumdecyloxy)benzaldehyde.
- A SMILES `O=Cc1cc(OCCCCCCCCCC[n+]2ccccc2)cc(OCCCCCCCCCC[n+]2ccccc2)c1` [high]; D the analogous 3,4-isomer.
- Questions: (a) draw the products: i. A + C (2:1); ii. C + D (1:2); iii. A + B (2:1); iv. B + D (1:2); (b) the relationship between the products of iii and iv.
- Answers: (a) bis-imines ArCH=N–linker–N=CHAr. (b) iii and iv are constitutional isomers (3,5- vs 3,4-substitution; same formula). [high]

#### 2.77 (book p108, PDF p16) — footnote 11 → J. Am. Chem. Soc. 2009, 131, 11478–11484
- Amino-acid-derived bolaamphiphile gelator: Pro-Val-NH–(CH2)8–NH-Val-Pro (L-prolyl-L-valyl amide on both ends of a 1,8-diaminooctane), drawn with stereo wedges.
- SMILES `CC(C)[C@H](NC(=O)[C@@H]1CCCN1)C(=O)NCCCCCCCCNC(=O)[C@@H](NC(=O)[C@@H]1CCCN1)C(C)C` [med; spacer counted as 8 CH2 from the zigzag; L-configurations assumed from the wedges].
- Questions: (a) all six N–H's can H-bond, but amide N–H's are stronger donors than amine N–H's; explain with resonance; (b) draw the intermolecular H-bonds in a stack of 3 molecules: 8 H-bonds from the central molecule's amide H's/O's to the two neighbors.
- Answer (a): resonance puts δ+ on the amide N (N⁺=C–O⁻), making N–H more polarized/acidic. [high]

#### 2.78 (book p108, PDF p16) — footnote 12 → J. Org. Chem. 1997, 62, 3200–3207
- Tropolone **1** (2-hydroxycyclohepta-2,4,6-trien-1-one, `O=c1cccccc1O`, Kekulé `O=C1C=CC=CC=C1O`) is acidic (→ tropolonate anion **2**) and basic (→ cation **3**, 1,2-dihydroxytropylium).
- Questions: (a) resonance structures of 2 and 3, and why they are stable; (b) C–C bond lengths alternate in 1 but are similar in 2 and 3; explain; (c) the intramolecular H-bond in 3 is much weaker than in 1; explain.
- Answers: (a) 2: the − charge is delocalized over both O's and the ring; 3: the + is delocalized onto ring carbons, giving an aromatic tropylium (6π) character; (b) delocalization equalizes the bonds (aromatic tropylium/tropolonate character); (c) in 3 both O's are OH donors (no good acceptor lone pair/C=O). The O's bear δ+, making them poor acceptors. [med-high]

#### 2.79 (book p108, PDF p16) — footnote 13 → Chem. Rev. 1993, 93, 381–433
- Basic red 1 (rhodamine 6G): xanthene with 3-NHEt/6-=N⁺HEt, 2,7-dimethyl, 9-(2-ethoxycarbonylphenyl); Cl⁻. The text refers to "문제 2.74" dyes, actually the triarylmethane dyes of 2.72.
- SMILES `CCNc1cc2oc3cc(=[NH+]CC)c(C)cc3c(-c3ccccc3C(=O)OCC)c2cc1C.[Cl-]` [high].
- Question: one of the four rings does not participate effectively in resonance (little positive charge); identify it and explain.
- Answer: the pendant 2-(ethoxycarbonyl)phenyl ring. It is twisted roughly perpendicular to the xanthene plane by steric clash (ortho ester + xanthene H's), so its p orbitals cannot overlap; also, putting + on the ring next to the electron-withdrawing ester is unfavorable. [high]

#### 2.80 (book p108, PDF p16) — footnote 14 → J. Invest. Dermatol. 2014, 134, 2988–2990
- Tofacitinib (JAK inhibitor; approved for psoriasis/RA; promotes hair regrowth). SMILES `CC1CCN(C(=O)CC#N)CC1N(C)c1ncnc2[nH]ccc12` (drug is (3R,4R)) [high].
- Task: identify every carbon and fill in the missing H count.
- Answer: C16H20N6O; H per carbon: CH3 (ring Me) 3, C4-H 1, C5 H2, C6 H2, C2 H2, C3-H 1, N-CH3 3, C=O 0, CH2CN 2, CN 0, pyrimidine C2-H 1, C4 0, C4a 0, C7a 0, pyrrole C5-H 1, C6-H 1; NH 1. [high]

## Chapter 3 — 산과 염기 (Acids and Bases)  (PDF p17–24, book p139, 141–147; book p140 is NOT in the PDF)

### Key concepts (개념과 용어의 복습 §3.1–3.9 + skill builders)
- §3.1 Brønsted–Lowry acid = proton donor, base = proton acceptor; gives conjugate acid + conjugate base.
- §3.2 Curved arrows show mechanism; proton transfer always uses at least two curved arrows.
- §3.3 Equilibria are described by Ka (pKa) rather than Keq; typical pKa −10 to 50; strong acid = low pKa. The equilibrium favors the weaker acid (higher pKa).
- §3.4 Qualitative acidity from conjugate-base stability: ARIO, i.e. (1) Atom (electronegativity within a row, size within a group), (2) Resonance, (3) Induction (e.g. halogens), (4) Orbital (sp > sp2 > sp3 carbanion stability). ARIO is the usual priority, with exceptions.
- §3.5 The equilibrium favors the more stable negative charge.
- §3.6 Leveling effect: in basic solution no base stronger than the solvent's conjugate base; in acidic solution no acid stronger than the solvent's conjugate acid.
- §3.7 Solvent effects (e.g. large/bulky bases are less effectively solvated) explain some pKa differences.
- §3.8 Counter-ions (cations) accompany negatively charged bases; their choice rarely matters in this book.
- §3.9 Lewis acid = electron-pair acceptor; Lewis base = electron-pair donor.
- Skill builders 3.5–3.12: ranking conjugate-base stability by atom/resonance/induction/orbital; combining all four (ARIO exceptions); predicting equilibrium without pKa; choosing reagents for proton transfer (water is not a suitable proton source for carboxylate); identifying Lewis acids/bases (BH3·THF).
- In-chapter problem 3.33 (book p139; 학습능력 응용): Nazarov cyclization, the dienone 1 (2-(3,4-dihydro-2H-pyran-6-yl) enone with α-Me, β-Ph) + AlCl3 → cyclopentenone 2; draw resonance of the carbonyl–AlCl3 Lewis complex. **No footnote marker** visible.

#### 3.62 (book p145, PDF p22) — footnote 2 → Tetrahedron Lett. 2012, 53, 5749–5752
- Total synthesis of (−)-seimatopolide A (antidiabetic). Acid: an oxonium ion, i.e. a secondary alkyl O bearing both an H⁺ and a MOM group (R–O⁺(H)–CH2OCH3), on a chain bearing OTBS (alkyl chain drawn as "CH3CH2(CH2)7"), a second OMOM, and a terminal vinyl. Base: Hünig's base (N,N-diisopropylethylamine, `CCN(C(C)C)C(C)C`).
- Substrate SMILES (stereo omitted; chain length per drawing) `CCCCCCCCCC(O[Si](C)(C)C(C)(C)C)CC(OCOC)C(C=C)[OH+]COC` [med].
- Questions: (a) identify the acid and base, draw the products with mechanism; (b) predict the equilibrium position using the pKa table.
- Answers: the base N removes the O–H proton → neutral MOM ether + iPr2EtNH⁺. Oxonium pKa ≈ −2 to −3.5 vs trialkylammonium ≈ 10–11, so the equilibrium lies far to the products. [high] (This is the last step of MOMCl/DIPEA protection.)

#### 3.63 (book p145, PDF p22) — footnote 3 → J. Am. Chem. Soc. 2009, 131, 8720–8721 (as printed; see swap note)
- Asteltoxin (from Aspergillus stellatus; inhibitor of E. coli BF1-ATPase); S. L. Schreiber synthesis. **1** = 4-methoxy-5,6-dimethyl-2H-pyran-2-one `COc1cc(=O)oc(C)c1C` [high]; strong base → anion **2** (deprotonated 6-CH3 → CH2⁻). Asteltoxin is shown: a bis-THF (furofuran) core linked by a triene to the 4-methoxy-5-methyl-2-pyrone.
- Question: draw all resonance structures of anion 2 and identify the major contributor.
- Answer: four contributors, with − on: exocyclic CH2; ring C5 (methyl-bearing carbon); C3 (α to C=O); carbonyl O (enolate). Major contributor: − on the carbonyl oxygen (most electronegative atom). [high]

#### 3.64 (book p146, PDF p23) — footnote 4 → J. Am. Chem. Soc. 1984, 106, 4186–4188 (as printed; see swap note)
- Compound designed for site-specific protein labeling: Nε-(propargyloxycarbonyl)-L-lysine. SMILES `N[C@@H](CCCCNC(=O)OCC#C)C(=O)O` [high; L from the hashed CO2H].
- Question: identify the four most acidic protons and rank them by increasing acidity.
- Answer: amine N–H (pKa ~38) < terminal alkyne C–H (~25) < carbamate N–H (~17–20, amide-like) < CO2H (~4–5). [med: the carbamate NH vs alkyne CH order depends on the book's table; a carbamate is amide-like (~17), so it is more acidic than the alkyne]

#### 3.65 (IR problem; book p146, PDF p23) — footnote 5 (on part b) → J. Chem. Ed. 1981, 58, 79–80
- Deuterium labeling: isobutane **1** → (Br2, hν) tert-butyl bromide **2** → (Mg, Et2O) t-BuMgBr **3** → (D2O) 2-deuterio-2-methylpropane **4**. SMILES: `CC(C)C`, `CC(C)(C)Br`, `CC(C)(C)[Mg]Br`, `[2H]C(C)(C)C` [high].
- Questions: (a) redraw 3 as the ion pair t-Bu⁻ BrMg⁺ and show the mechanism 3 → 4 (carbanion deprotonates D2O → C–D + DO⁻); (b) IR of 4: signals at 1250–1500, 2180, and 2800–3000 cm⁻¹; locate the C–D stretch and justify.
- Answer: C–D at 2180 cm⁻¹. ν ∝ √(k/μ) and D roughly doubles the reduced mass, so ν(C–D) ≈ ν(C–H)/√2 ≈ 2100–2200 cm⁻¹. [high]

#### 3.66 (IR problem; book p146, PDF p23) — footnote 6 → Tetrahedron Lett. 2007, 48, 8787–8789
- Bengamide synthesis (methionine aminopeptidase inhibitors). **1** = a 1,2-di-O-benzoyl polyol: BzO–CH2–CH(OBz)–CH(OH)–CH(OTMS)–CH(OMe)–CH=CH–Ph (E-styryl). **2** = TMSCl; intermediate **3** = O-silyl oxonium (R–O⁺(H)SiMe3); base **4** = 2,6-lutidine; product **5** = TMS ether.
- SMILES 1 (stereo omitted) `O=C(OCC(OC(=O)c1ccccc1)C(O)C(O[Si](C)(C)C)C(OC)/C=C/c1ccccc1)c1ccccc1` [med].
- Questions: (a) draw 5 and the mechanism from 3 (lutidine removes O–H⁺); (b) use pKa to judge whether 4 is a suitable base (oxonium ~ −2 to −3 vs 2,6-lutidinium ~6.7, so yes, the equilibrium strongly favors 5; lutidine is hindered and non-nucleophilic); (c) IR check 1 → 5: the broad O–H stretch (3200–3600 cm⁻¹) disappears. [high]

#### 3.67 (도전 문제; book p146, PDF p23) — footnote 7 → J. Org. Chem. 2009, 74, 2433–2437
- (+)-Coronafacic acid (core of the phytotoxin coronatine) synthesis: a bicyclo[3.1.0]hexan-2-one-1-carboxylic acid (−)-menthyl ester + 2,2-dimethylpropane-1,3-diol, p-TsOH (cat.), −H2O → 5,5-dimethyl-1,3-dioxane ketal (spiro), 89%.
- SMILES SM `O=C1CCC2CC12C(=O)OC1CC(C)CCC1C(C)C`; product `CC1(C)COC2(CCC3CC23C(=O)OC2CC(C)CCC2C(C)C)OC1` [med; stereo omitted].
- Questions: (a) curved-arrow mechanism of the first step: protonation of the ketone C=O by TsOH; (b) does the equilibrium favor the protonated ketone? Support with pKa.
- Answer: no. Protonated ketone pKa ≈ −7 vs TsOH ≈ −2.8 (or −1), so the equilibrium favors the neutral ketone + TsOH; only a small fraction is protonated (enough for catalysis). [high]

#### 3.68 (book p146, PDF p23) — footnote 8 → Org. Lett. 2002, 4, 2645–2648
- Phakellin (**3**, marine antibiotic natural product) synthesis. **1** = cyclo(L-Pro-L-Pro) diketopiperazine, `O=C1[C@@H]2CCCN2C(=O)[C@@H]2CCCN12` [high; cis H's wedged]; LDA → **2** (enolate) → … → **3** (pyrrole-fused tetracyclic 2-aminoimidazoline; SMILES uncertain).
- Questions: (a) the most acidic proton in 1 and its conjugate base 2 (α-C–H at the Pro α-carbon, next to the amide C=O; the enolate is resonance-delocalized onto O); (b) why LDA is suitable: quantitatively, diisopropylamine pKa ~36 vs α-H of an amide ~25–30, so it is favorable; qualitatively, it is bulky and non-nucleophilic and does not attack C=O; (c) mechanism 1 → 2. [high]

#### 3.69 (book p146–147) — no footnote (E. J. Corey ginkgolide B synthesis). Compound 1 is a spiro THF/cyclopentanone with tBu; LDA → enolate 3a↔3b; PhNTf2 (4) → enol triflate 5 + PhN(Tf)⁻Li⁺ (6). Listed for completeness.

#### 3.70 (book p147, PDF p24) — footnote 9 → J. Org. Chem. 1981, 46, 4327–4331 (Bordwell DMSO acidities)
- Data (most acidic CH2, DMSO pKa): CH3COCH2CO2CH2CH3 (1) 14.1; PhCH2CN (2) 21.9; PhCH2CO2CH2CH3 (3) 22.7; PhCH2CON(CH3)2 (4) 26.6.
- Question: compare conjugate bases of **5** diphenylmethane (`c1ccc(Cc2ccccc2)cc1`) and **6** phenylacetone (`CC(=O)Cc1ccccc1`); decide which is more acidic with all resonance contributors, and which contributor is more effective.
- Answer: 6 is much more acidic (DMSO pKa ≈ 19.9 vs 32.2). The enolate places − on oxygen (electronegative), which stabilizes it more than delocalization into a phenyl ring (− only on ring carbons). From the data, carbonyl/nitrile stabilization > phenyl. [high]

#### 3.71 (book p147, PDF p24) — footnote 10 → Tetrahedron Lett. 2012, 53, 4051–4053
- 7-Desmethoxyfusarentin synthesis (cytotoxic to MCF-7). Imidazole (`c1c[nH]cn1`) + oxonium of a 2,6-disubstituted tetrahydropyran-4-ol carrying O⁺(H)–TBS at C4 (2-propyl, 6-CH2OR).
- SMILES (stereo omitted) `CCCC1CC(CC(COR)O1)[OH+][Si](C)(C)C(C)(C)C` [med].
- Question: predict products and mechanism. Answer: imidazole's sp2 N deprotonates the O–H⁺ → TBS ether + imidazolium. Imidazolium pKa ~7 vs oxonium ~ −2, so the products are strongly favored. [high]

#### 3.72 (book p147, PDF p24) — footnote 11 → J. Am. Chem. Soc. 2009, 131, 8720–8721
- Same lysine derivative as 3.64 (`N[C@@H](CCCCNC(=O)OCC#C)C(=O)O`). Predict the products of 1 mol of compound with: (a–d) 1, 2, 3, 4 mol EtNa; (e–h) 1, 2, 3, 4 mol EtONa.
- Answer (EtNa: conjugate acid ethane pKa ~50, so it deprotonates all four sites in order of acidity): 1 eq → carboxylate; 2 eq → + carbamate N⁻; 3 eq → + acetylide; 4 eq → + amide (NH⁻) on the α-amine. EtONa (EtOH pKa ~16): only the CO2H is deprotonated irreversibly; more EtONa gives at most partial carbamate N–H deprotonation (pKa ~17, an equilibrium); the alkyne and amine are not deprotonated. [med; order of steps 2/3 as in 3.64]

#### 3.73 (book p147) — no footnote (piperidine alkaloid from Streptomyces; protonation at N, pKa data −2.2 vs 10.6 given). Listed for completeness.

## Chapter 4 — 알케인과 사이클로알케인 (Alkanes and Cycloalkanes)  (PDF p25–33, book p188–196)

### Key concepts (개념과 용어의 복습 §4.1–4.15 + skill builders 4.1–4.13)
- §4.1 Alkanes = saturated hydrocarbons; nomenclature.
- §4.2 IUPAC naming in four steps (parent incl. cycloalkanes; substituents, alkyl and complex/branched, with allowed common names; numbering/locants; alphabetical assembly with di/tri… ignored). Bicyclic naming: "bicyclo[x.y.z]" with bridge lengths in brackets; numbering starts at a bridgehead and follows the longest bridge.
- §4.3 Number of constitutional isomers grows with size; use IUPAC names to avoid duplicates.
- §4.4 Heat of combustion (−ΔH°) compares the stability of isomeric alkanes (branched = more stable).
- §4.5 Petroleum: fractional distillation, cracking, reforming (octane rating).
- §4.6–4.8 Conformations and Newman projections; dihedral/torsional angle; staggered (low) vs eclipsed (high); torsional strain (ethane ~12 kJ/mol; propane larger); butane anti < gauche (gauche steric interaction).
- §4.9 Cycloalkane angle strain + torsional strain; heat of combustion per CH2.
- §4.10 Cyclohexane chair (no torsional or angle strain); boat (flagpole + eclipsing), twist boat.
- §4.11–4.13 Axial/equatorial; ring flip; 1,3-diaxial interactions favor equatorial substituents; for polysubstituted rings, sum the axial energy costs (e.g. Et 8.0, Me 7.6, Cl 2.0 kJ/mol in skill builder 4.13).
- §4.14 cis/trans (clearest in Haworth projections).
- §4.15 cis- vs trans-decalin are stereoisomers (no ring-flip interconversion); norbornane = bicyclo[2.2.1]heptane; camphor, camphene; diamond lattice of chairs.

#### 4.67 (종합 문제; book p194–195, PDF p32) — footnote 1 (on "titin¹") → J. Am. Chem. Soc. 2009, 131, 8766–8768
- A structural unit used in synthetic polymers that mimic the backbone of the muscle protein titin: a 2-ureido-4(1H)-pyrimidinone (UPy) unit, shown as an N–C(=O)–NH–C2 urea attached to a pyrimidinone (ring N1–H, C4=O, ring N3), with ring substituents (wavy bonds) at C5 and C6. Core SMILES `O=C(N*)Nc1nc(=O)c(*)c(*)[nH]1` [med].
- Task: in its most stable conformation, identical units form one intramolecular H-bond and four intermolecular H-bonds; draw two such units showing all interactions.
- Answer: intramolecular NH(urea, terminal N–H)···N3 (ring) H-bond (6-membered pseudo-ring) makes the unit planar and presents a DDAA array (ring N1–H, inner urea N–H, urea C=O… / C4=O, N3). Two units dimerize through a self-complementary quadruple H-bond (Meijer UPy dimer). [med-high]

#### 4.68 (book p195, PDF p32) — footnote 2 (on "Aspergillus nomius²") → J. Am. Chem. Soc. 2012, 134, 8078–8081
- Aspernomine (polycyclic cytotoxic indole diterpenoid from Aspergillus nomius); a proposed biosynthetic route. Drawn: an indoline/aniline N–H fused carbocyclic skeleton with a cyclohexanone, two angular methyls (Me, Me) on adjacent ring-fusion carbons, a secondary OH, and a homoprenyl side chain CH2CH2CH=C(CH3)2 at a ring-fusion carbon. SMILES: uncertain (complex polycycle; not determined).
- Questions: (a) two six-membered rings are drawn as chairs; is their junction like cis- or trans-decalin? (b) Is the aromatic ring axial or equatorial? (c) For the 6-carbon acyclic substituent on a bridgehead carbon, is it axial or equatorial with respect to each chair? (d) The approximate dihedral angle between the two methyls on the chairs.
- Tentative answers [low; depend on 3D reading]: (a) trans-decalin-like (the angular groups anti); (d) ~60° (gauche, adjacent ring-fusion methyls in a trans-diaxial-avoiding arrangement), or 180° if both are axial on a trans junction. **I could not verify from the 2D drawing; consult the original.**

#### 4.69 (도전 문제; book p195, PDF p32) — footnote 3 (on "수소 결합 상호작용³") → J. Am. Chem. Soc. 2009, 131, 8766–8768
- Butane-2,3-diol, drawn as a sawhorse/zigzag along C2–C3. My CIP assignment from the wedges is (2R,3R): C2 has H hashed, CH3 and OH in plane; C3 has HO wedged, H hashed, CH3 in plane. SMILES `C[C@@H](O)[C@H](C)O` = (R,R) [med for the stereo reading].
- Task: draw the three staggered conformers about C2–C3 (Newman); considering gauche interactions and hydrogen bonding, decide the most stable.
- Answer: the conformer with CH3/CH3 anti and OH/OH gauche (allows an intramolecular O–H···O hydrogen bond) is most stable. [med-high]

#### 4.70 (book p195, PDF p32) — footnote 4 → J. Phys. Chem. A 2002, 106, 1642–1646
- Methanol rotation about C–O: staggered vs eclipsed; barrier ≈ 4.2 kJ/mol (1 kcal/mol).
- Questions: (a) Newman projections of both; (b) the energy cost of a lone-pair/C–H eclipsing interaction.
- Answer: the eclipsed form has one H/H eclipse (~4.0 kJ/mol, from ethane 12/3) + two lone-pair/C–H eclipses. 4.2 − 4.0 = 0.2 kJ/mol, so ~0.1 kJ/mol each (essentially negligible). [high]

#### 4.71 (book p195–196, PDF p32–33) — footnote 5 → J. Org. Chem. 2012, 77, 7479–7486
- Fluorophore: 1,4-bis(1-R-1H-1,2,3-triazol-4-yl)phenazine (R = polyether). Planar conformers A and B differ by 180° rotation of each triazole about its aryl–triazole C–C bond. SMILES (R = Me placeholder) `Cn1cc(-c2ccc(-c3cn(C)nn3)c3nc4ccccc4nc23)nn1` [high].
- Questions: (a) which σ bonds must rotate for A → B, and what happens to the geometry during rotation (the triazoles twist out of the phenazine plane, temporarily breaking π-conjugation); (b) A is 8.96 kcal/mol more stable than B; explain.
- Answer (b): in A, the triazole C5–H points at the phenazine N (stabilizing C–H···N interaction) and the triazole N lone pairs point away. In B, the triazole N2/N3 lone pairs face the phenazine N lone pairs (electrostatic lone-pair repulsion), and the R groups/H interact sterically. [med]

#### 4.72 (book p196, PDF p33) — footnote 6 (on "모두 수평방향 형태⁶") → J. Am. Chem. Soc. 1990, 112, 893–894
- all-trans-1,2,3,4,5,6-hexaethylcyclohexane **1** adopts the all-equatorial chair; all-trans-1,2,3,4,5,6-hexaisopropylcyclohexane **2** has a very unstable all-equatorial form and prefers all-axial.
- SMILES 1 (stereo omitted) `CCC1C(CC)C(CC)C(CC)C(CC)C1CC`; 2 `CC(C)C1C(C(C)C)C(C(C)C)C(C(C)C)C(C(C)C)C1C(C)C` [high constitution].
- Questions: (a) why adjacent equatorial iPr groups clash severely while Et groups don't; draw the all-equatorial chair of 2 and a Newman projection down the ring–CH(CH3)2 bond; (b) why the all-axial chair of 2 is lower in energy (Newman down the C(ring)–iPr bond).
- Answers: (a) Et can rotate its CH3 away so that only H's face the neighbor. iPr has two CH3's, so in any rotamer one methyl is gauche to/eclipses the adjacent equatorial iPr (gear clash; each substituent is flanked by two neighbors). (b) In the all-axial form, alternating up/down axial iPr groups are 1,3-related only to other iPr groups. Each iPr can orient its methine H toward the ring interior/axial neighbors and its methyls outward, avoiding the vicinal gauche clashes, so it is less strained overall. [med-high]

#### 4.73 (book p196, PDF p33) — footnote 7 (on "titin⁷") → J. Am. Chem. Soc. 2009, 131, 8766–8768
- Same ureidopyrimidinone (UPy) concept as 4.67, drawn as R/R'-substituted 2-(3-R''-ureido)pyrimidin-4(1H)-one (ring N1–H, C4=O, N3; urea NH–C(=O)–NHR''). SMILES `O=C(NR'')Nc1nc(=O)c(R)c(R')[nH]1` [med].
- Task and answer as in 4.67: one intramolecular H-bond (terminal urea N–H···N3) plus four intermolecular H-bonds in the dimer (quadruple DDAA·AADD). [med-high]

#### 4.74 (book p196, PDF p33) — footnote 8 → J. Am. Chem. Soc. 1961, 83, 606–614
- Two diastereomeric γ-lactones fused to a trans-decalin (linear tricyclic: decalin + γ-butyrolactone on the 2,3-positions of ring B). Fusion H's: top wedge, bottom hash (trans-decalin) in both. Compound 1: ring-B C–CH2 bond hashed, C–O wedge. Compound 2: C–CH2 wedge, C–O hashed (each lactone itself trans-fused). The heats of combustion differ by 17.2 kJ/mol.
- SMILES (constitution) `O=C1CC2CC3CCCCC3CC2O1` [high]; stereo omitted.
- Questions: (a) redraw 1 and 2 with chairs and their lowest-energy conformers; (b) which has the larger heat of combustion, and why?
- Answer (my configurational analysis): in the rigid trans-decalin, 1 has both lactone ring bonds equatorial (a trans-diequatorial 1,2-fusion, strain-free chair). In 2 the lactone bonds would be trans-diaxial (180° apart), which a 5-membered ring cannot span. Ring B cannot flip (trans-decalin), so it must adopt a twist-boat. **2 has the larger heat of combustion** (17.2 kJ/mol less stable). [med-high]

#### 4.75 (book p196, PDF p33) — footnote 9 (on "비타민 D의 활성⁹") → Bioorg. Med. Chem. Lett. 2012, 22, 1756–1760
- Vitamin D mimics: R–O–CH2–(bicyclo[2.2.2]octan-1-yl)–(CH2)4–C(OH)(CH2CH3)2. The highlighted hydrocarbon core = bicyclo[2.2.2]octane + CH2 (methyl) + the 5-ethylheptyl chain (OH and OR excluded).
- SMILES of the whole (R = H) `OCC12CCC(CCCCC(O)(CC)CC)(CC1)CC2` [med; chain counted as 4 CH2 then C(OH)].
- Task: give the systematic name of the hydrocarbon core.
- Answer: **1-(5-ethylheptyl)-4-methylbicyclo[2.2.2]octane** [med; depends on the chain count].

(4.36–4.66 have no literature footnotes.)

## Chapter 5 — 입체이성질현상 (Stereoisomerism)  (PDF p34–41, book p230–237)

### Key concepts (개념과 용어의 복습 §5.1–5.10 + skill builders 5.1–5.9)
- §5.1 Constitutional isomers vs stereoisomers; cis/trans for disubstituted cycloalkanes and alkenes.
- §5.2 Chirality = non-superimposable mirror image; chirality center (four different groups); one center → enantiomers.
- §5.3 CIP R/S: prioritize by atomic number, lowest priority on a dash, 1→2→3 clockwise = R.
- §5.4 Polarimetry; optically active; specific rotation [α] = α/(c·l); dextrorotatory (+)/levorotatory (−); racemic mixture; enantiomeric excess.
- §5.5 Diastereomers; at most 2ⁿ stereoisomers.
- §5.6 Rotational vs reflectional symmetry; a plane of symmetry → achiral; meso compounds (fewer than 2ⁿ stereoisomers).
- §5.7 Fischer projections (horizontal = wedges, vertical = dashes).
- §5.8 Conformationally mobile compounds (butane, cis-1,2-dimethylcyclohexane) are optically inactive (rapid interconversion of enantiomeric conformers).
- §5.9 A chirality center is not required for chirality: atropisomers (hindered rotation), allenes.
- §5.10 Resolution with chiral resolving agents and chiral column chromatography.
- Skill builders: cis/trans; locating chirality centers; drawing enantiomers; CIP assignment; specific rotation; % ee; enantiomer vs diastereomer; meso; Fischer → R/S.

## Chapter 6 — 화학반응성과 메커니즘 (Chemical Reactivity and Mechanisms)  (PDF p42–52, book p274–284)

### Key concepts (개념과 용어의 복습 §6.1–6.12 + skill builders 6.1–6.6)
- §6.1 Enthalpy ΔH (heat of reaction); bond dissociation energy (homolytic cleavage); exothermic vs endothermic.
- §6.2 Entropy; spontaneity requires ΔS_total > 0; ΔS_sys > 0 when the number of molecules or conformational freedom increases.
- §6.3 ΔG < 0 = exergonic (spontaneous); ΔG > 0 = endergonic.
- §6.4 Equilibrium: Keq relates to ΔG (ΔG < 0 → Keq > 1); thermodynamics.
- §6.5 Kinetics: rate equations (1st/2nd/3rd order); activation energy Ea; temperature and catalysts.
- §6.6 Energy diagrams: transition states (maxima), intermediates (minima); Hammond postulate.
- §6.7 Polar (ionic) reactions: nucleophiles (lone pairs, π bonds, inductive effects), electrophiles (inductive δ+, empty p orbital, carbocations); polarizability.
- §6.8 Curved arrows: four arrow-pushing patterns: (1) nucleophilic attack, (2) loss of a leaving group, (3) proton transfer, (4) rearrangement (hydride/methyl shift). Carbocation stability 3° > 2° > 1° (hyperconjugation).
- §6.9 Every ionic mechanism combines these four patterns; concerted steps use two patterns at once.
- §6.10 Arrow rules: tail on a bond or lone pair; head to form a bond or lone pair; never give a 2nd-row element (C, N, O, F) a fifth orbital/expanded octet.
- §6.11 Carbocation rearrangements occur only when they give a more stable cation (none from 3° or resonance-stabilized allylic cations).
- §6.12 Irreversible arrows: poor leaving group + strong nucleophile; proton transfers with a large pKa difference; carbocation rearrangements. Otherwise use equilibrium arrows.
- Skill builders: ΔH° from BDEs (example: isobutane + Cl2 → tert-butyl chloride + HCl, ΔH° = −138 kJ/mol); identifying nucleophilic/electrophilic centers; arrow-pushing patterns and their sequences; drawing curved arrows; predicting carbocation rearrangements.

#### 6.52 (book p282) — NO footnote visible. Listed for context only.
α,β-Epoxy ketone N-chlorohydrazone (2,3-epoxy-3-methylcyclohexanone-derived, N–N(H)Cl) + HO⁻ → vinyl azo (N=N–Cl) alkoxide → hept-6-yn-2-one (`CC(=O)CCCC#C`) + N2 + Cl⁻. Draw the curved arrows (Eschenmoser–Tanabe-type fragmentation).

#### 6.53 (book p282, PDF p50) — footnote 2 → J. Am. Chem. Soc. 2012, 134, 17877–17880
- Base-catalyzed (cat. MeO⁻ in MeOH) rearrangement of **1** into the propellane isomer **5**. 1 = a tricyclic butenolide: a cyclopentene-fused α,β-unsaturated γ-lactone (butenolide) whose spiro/quaternary γ-carbon carries a CH2CH2–O bridge forming a tetrahydrofuran to a bridgehead CH (oxa-bridged).
- Steps shown: 1 + MeO⁻ ⇌ **2** (carbanion at the allylic bridgehead CH γ to the butenolide; extended dienolate; major contributor shown separately) ⇌ **3** (β-elimination: ring C–O bond breaks → cyclopentadiene-fused butenolide + pendant CH2CH2O⁻) ⇌ **4** (intramolecular oxa-Michael addition of the alkoxide to the diene/butenolide, giving a lactone enolate; new THF ring) ⇌ (MeOH) **5** (protonation, regenerating MeO⁻).
- Task: curved arrows for each step and the major resonance contributor of 2.
- Answers: 1→2 proton transfer (MeO⁻ removes the γ/allylic H, which is acidic because the anion is a vinylogous enolate). The major contributor of 2 has the − charge on the lactone carbonyl oxygen (dienolate O⁻). 2→3: loss of a leaving group (E1cB-type ring opening expelling alkoxide). 3→4: nucleophilic attack (conjugate addition). 4→5: proton transfer from MeOH, regenerating the catalyst. [med-high]
- SMILES: not determined with confidence (cage connectivity) — **uncertain**.

#### 6.54 (book p282, PDF p50) — footnote 3 → J. Org. Chem. 2007, 72, 793–798
- Probe of an intramolecular elimination (stereochemistry with D labels). **1** = tert-butyl 3-acetoxy-2,3-dideuteriobutanoate (C3 bears CH3, D, OAc; C2 bears H, D, CO2tBu; specific stereo drawn with wedges/dashes).
- SMILES (stereo omitted) `[2H]C(C)(OC(C)=O)C([2H])C(=O)OC(C)(C)C` [high constitution].
- Proposed path: HO⁻ adds to the acetate C=O → tetrahedral alkoxide ⇌ concerted syn-elimination: the alkoxide O⁻ removes the α-H through a cyclic TS, C=C forms and the C3–O bond breaks, releasing acetic acid (as the hydrate/orthoacid, losing HO⁻) and tert-butyl (E)-2,3-dideuteriobut-2-enoate (`C/C([2H])=C([2H])/C(=O)OC(C)(C)C`, with the D's trans to each other as drawn). Then HO⁻ + AcOH → AcO⁻ + H2O.
- Task: complete the mechanism with curved arrows matching each step's bond changes.
- Answer: as above. The patterns are nucleophilic attack; then concerted proton transfer + loss of the leaving group (intramolecular, syn); then proton transfer. The D-labeling reveals the syn geometry. [med-high]

#### 6.55 (book p282–283, PDF p50–51) — footnote 4 (on "균류⁴", coprophilous fungi) → Org. Lett. 1999, 1, 3–5
- Biomimetic synthesis inspired by preussomerins (antifungals from dung-loving fungi). SM: a spiroketal linking a tetrahydronaphthoquinone (enedione ring) to naphthalene-1,8-diol, with the naphthalene bearing a para-OH (4-hydroxy). LiOH → naphthoxide → (one step) rearranged bis-spiroketal anion: the former quinone ring becomes an aromatic hydroquinone mono-anion, and the naphthalene ring becomes a 4-oxo-cyclohexadienone whose C1 is a new ketal carbon bonded to the former quinone oxygen. Then H2O protonates the phenoxide.
- Questions: (a) curved arrows for each step; (b) explain the observed stereochemistry.
- Answers: (a) deprotonation (proton transfer); an intramolecular redox-type step in which the electron-rich naphthoxide pushes electrons (O⁻ → C4=O) and the quinone oxygen becomes nucleophilic, forming the new C1–O ketal bond and aromatizing the quinone ring to a hydroquinone anion; then protonation. [low: the single polar arrow set is not obvious; it may be stepwise via SET; consult the paper]. (b) The new O–C1 bond forms intramolecularly on the only face the rigid spiro framework allows, so a single diastereomer (the cis-fused bis-ketal shown with wedges) results. [med]

#### 6.56 (book p283, PDF p51) — footnote 5 → Org. Lett. 2012, 14, 4710–4713
- (+)-Aureol biosynthesis (selective anticancer activity against certain lung and colon cancers): carbocation **A** → carbocation **B**. A is a trans-decalin (gem-dimethyl at C4, angular CH3 at C10) with the cation at C8 (bearing CH3), adjacent to C9, which bears CH2-(2,5-dihydroxyphenyl). B has the cation at C10 (ring fusion); C9 now carries CH3 + CH2Ar; C8 carries H + CH3 (stereo per wedges).
- Answer (mechanism): (1) a 1,2-hydride shift from C9 to C8 (cation now at C9, tertiary) then (2) a 1,2-methyl shift of the angular CH3 from C10 to C9 → tertiary cation at C10 = B (a "friedo"-type backbone rearrangement). Each migrating group moves suprafacially on the face it leaves from: the H and CH3 are trans-diaxial/antiperiplanar to the empty p orbital. This sets the observed relative configurations (the C8 methyl and the C9 methyl/benzyl relationships). [med-high]
- SMILES: uncertain for full stereo; A constitution `CC1(C)CCCC2(C)C1CC[C+](C)C2Cc1cc(O)ccc1O` [med].

#### 6.57 (book p283, PDF p51) — footnote 6 → J. Am. Chem. Soc. 1954, 76, 4749–4751 (Woodward, total synthesis of strychnine)
- Last stage of Woodward's synthesis: isostrychnine-type **1** (α,β-unsaturated lactam + allylic CH2OH) with HO⁻ → **2** (deprotonation at the γ-C–H giving an extended lactam dienolate) → H2O → **3** (reprotonation at the α-carbon, moving the C=C into conjugation in a new position) → HO⁻ → **4** (alkoxide by O–H deprotonation) → **5** (intramolecular oxa-Michael addition forms the ether ring; lactam enolate) → H2O → **6** (−)-strychnine.
- Strychnine SMILES `O=C1C[C@H]2OCC=C3CN4CC[C@]56[C@@H]4C[C@H]3[C@H]2[C@H]5N1c1ccccc16` [med; standard literature SMILES].
- Questions: (a) all curved arrows and the arrow-pushing patterns 1→6 (proton transfer, proton transfer, proton transfer, nucleophilic attack (conjugate addition), proton transfer); (b) for 4→5, identify the nucleophilic center (alkoxide O) and the electrophilic center (β-carbon of the α,β-unsaturated lactam), and justify the electrophile's electron deficiency via resonance (C=C–C=O ↔ ⁺C–C=C–O⁻); (c) 4→5 creates a new chirality center; give its configuration and why the other is not observed.
- Answer (c): the configuration is the one in natural strychnine (the ether O on the face set by the cage). The alkoxide can reach only one face of the alkene intramolecularly; the other configuration would require an impossibly strained ring. [med; R/S not assigned]

#### 6.58 (도전 문제; book p283, PDF p51) — footnote 7 (on "Isocoma wrightii⁷") → J. Am. Chem. Soc. 1979, 101, 7130–7131 (Pirrung, isocomene)
- Isocomene (sesquiterpene, triquinane): a carbocation rearrangement that is neither a methyl nor a hydride shift. SM cation: a tertiary cation on a 6-membered ring fused to a cyclobutane (bicyclo[4.2.0]) that is spiro/fused to a cyclopentane, with methyl groups → product: a triquinane tertiary cation (angular triquinane skeleton).
- Questions: (a) identify the migrating carbon and draw the mechanism; (b) a new chirality center forms with only one configuration (diastereoselective because two diastereomeric cations are possible); draw the unformed diastereomer and explain why it is unfavorable (hint: models); (c) the cation goes from tertiary to tertiary; suggest the driving force.
- Answers: (a) a 1,2-alkyl shift: a cyclobutane ring C–C bond (the carbon bonded to the cation-adjacent carbon) migrates to the cation, i.e. a ring expansion of a cyclobutylcarbinyl cation to a cyclopentyl cation; (b) the migrating bond must be aligned (antiperiplanar) with the empty p orbital; the alternative diastereomer would need the bond on the other face, giving a strained trans-fused bicyclo[3.3.0] (diquinane) system; (c) relief of cyclobutane ring strain (4- → 5-membered ring). [med-high]

#### 6.59 (book p283–284, PDF p51–52) — footnote 8 → Org. Lett. 1999, 1, 537–539
- Thermal N2 loss (250 °C) from a 4-amino-pyrrolo-fused 1,2,3-triazine **1** (R1/R2-substituted pyrrole) gives 2-amino-3-cyanopyrrole **4**. Proposed (later refuted) mechanism: via **2** (ring-opened diazonium/amidine anion: C(=NH)–N⁻H … pyrrole–N≡N⁺) and **3** (a fused azetine: 2-imino-azetidine/amidine four-membered ring), then ring opening to 4. 15N-labeling: 1 with ¹⁵NH2 → 4a (¹⁵N in the nitrile, ¹⁵N≡C–) observed; 4b (¹⁵N in the NH2) not observed.
- Questions: (a) curved arrows for 2 → 3 (the amide-type N⁻ attacks the C bearing N2⁺, with loss of N2; four-membered ring formation); (b) explain with resonance why the proposed mechanism predicts formation of 4b.
- Answer (b): in 3 the exocyclic =NH and the ring NH form an amidine (H–N=C–NH ↔ ⁻/⁺ resonance, plus tautomerization), making the two N atoms equivalent. Ring opening could then put either N in the nitrile, so a ~1:1 mixture of 4a and 4b would be expected. The absence of 4b rules this path out. [high]
- Core SMILES of 4 (R1 = R2 = H placeholder) `Nc1[nH]ccc1C#N` [high].

#### 6.60 (book p284, PDF p52) — footnote 9 → Org. Lett. 2005, 7, 515–517
- Lewis-acid-promoted rearrangement: **1** = 6-methyl-2-phenyl-1,3-dioxane bearing a spiro-cyclopropane at C4 (cyclopropanone ketal-type), `CC1CC2(CC2)OC(c2ccccc2)O1` [med-high] → **2** = ring-opened oxocarbenium (PhCH=O⁺–CH(Me)–) with a cyclopropanolate (cyclopropyl–O⁻ on the spiro carbon) → **3** = cis-2-methyl-7-phenyloxepan-4-one, `CC1CC(=O)CCC(c2ccccc2)O1` (drawn 2,7-cis with wedges) [high constitution].
- Questions: (a) arrows for 1 → 2 (the Lewis acid coordinates O; the acetal C–O bond to the cyclopropyl-bearing O breaks, giving an oxocarbenium + cyclopropoxide); (b) arrows for 2 → 3 (hint: redraw 2).
- Answer (b): the cyclopropanolate acts as a homoenolate. The O⁻ lone pair re-forms C=O, the strained cyclopropane C–C bond breaks, and the resulting CH2 nucleophile (σ bond) attacks the oxocarbenium carbon, closing the 7-membered oxepanone (relief of cyclopropane strain). Redraw 2 so that the CH2 is near the C=O⁺ carbon. [high]

#### 6.61 (book p284, PDF p52) — footnote 10 → Org. Biomol. Chem. 2013, 11, 2498–2513
- Natural products from Sarcostemma acidum (folk antitussive); a strong acid (TsOH) converts one natural product into another by cyclization. Model: **1** = 2,3-dimethyl-4,4-diphenylbut-3-en-1-ol, `CC(CO)C(C)=C(c1ccccc1)c1ccccc1` [high] → TsOH → **2** = 2,3-dimethyl-4-phenyl-1,2-dihydronaphthalene, `CC1Cc2ccccc2C(c2ccccc2)=C1C` [high].
- Task: identify the sequence of arrow-pushing patterns.
- Answer: (1) proton transfer (TsOH protonates OH → –OH2⁺); (2) concerted nucleophilic attack by the phenyl π bond + loss of the leaving group (H2O); intramolecular Friedel–Crafts-type SN2 at the primary carbon gives an arenium (σ-complex) cation; (3) proton transfer (H2O removes the ring H, restoring aromaticity). [high]

(6.19–6.51 have no literature footnotes.)

## Chapter 7 — 치환반응 (Substitution Reactions)  (book pp. 333–341; PDF p-01 … p-09)

### Key reactions / concepts from summary (반응의 복습, p-01–p-04)
- **SN2**: concerted, backside attack, rate = k[substrate][Nu]; methyl > 1° > 2° > 3°; inversion of configuration (stereospecific). Favoured by strong Nu, good LG, polar aprotic solvent (DMSO, DMF). E.g. (S)-2-bromobutane + HO⁻ → (R)-2-butanol; EtCl + NaSH → EtSH.
- **SN1**: stepwise (LG loss = rate-determining → carbocation → Nu attack), rate = k[substrate]; 3° > 2° > 1° > methyl; racemization (slight excess inversion due to ion pair). Favoured by weak Nu, excellent LG, protic solvent. Possible extra steps: proton transfer before LG loss (OH → OH2⁺), carbocation rearrangement, deprotonation after neutral Nu attack (solvolysis). E.g. 3° bromide + NaCN → pair of enantiomers.
- **Activating OH**: ROH + TsCl/pyridine → ROTs (retention), then NaCN/DMSO (SN2, inversion); ROH + HX (SN1 for 3°).
- Leaving groups: halides, sulfonates (tosylate most common).

#### 7.77 — cyoctol (anti-androgen / male-pattern baldness agent), fn 1, Tetrahedron 2004, 60, 9599–9614 (종합 문제, p.339)
- SM: (R)-3-methoxypentan-1-ol (OMe on wedge), `CC[C@@H](OC)CCO` [H for constitution; R assigned from drawing, M].
- Conditions: 1) TsCl, pyridine; 2) NaI; 3) PPh3, heat → "?".
- Asks: (a) draw the products of the sequence and explain why step 3 is favourable (a neutral nucleophile gives an ion pair as product); (b) rationale for step 2.
- Answer [H]: 1) primary tosylate (C–O retained); 2) Finkelstein SN2 → primary iodide (I⁻ better LG / better substrate for weakly basic neutral PPh3, NaOTs precipitates/acetone-type driving force); 3) SN2 by PPh3 → (R)-(3-methoxypentyl)triphenylphosphonium iodide `CC[C@@H](OC)CC[P+](c1ccccc1)(c1ccccc1)c1ccccc1.[I-]` (Wittig salt). Stereocentre untouched (reaction at C1). Step 3 favoured: P is a large, polarizable, strong neutral nucleophile; primary unhindered carbon; iodide excellent LG.
- Concept: activating OH as sulfonate; Finkelstein; SN2 with neutral Nu → phosphonium salt.

#### 7.78 — 2-naphthol Williamson ether synthesis (IR-monitored teaching lab), fn 2, J. Chem. Ed. 2009, 86, 850–852
- SM 1: 2-naphthol `Oc1ccc2ccccc2c1`; NaOH → 2 (sodium 2-naphthoxide); then n-butyl iodide → 3.
- Asks: (a) structure/mechanism for 2, and justify why hydroxide is basic enough (pKa naphthol ≈ 9.5 vs water 15.7 → equilibrium lies far to phenoxide); (b) structure/mechanism of 3.
- Answer [H]: 3 = 2-butoxynaphthalene `CCCCOc1ccc2ccccc2c1` (C14H16O) via SN2 of naphthoxide on 1° BuI. (c) on p-07: product purified by recrystallization from water; must be dried before IR because water O–H band would mask disappearance of the phenol O–H; complete conversion shown by absence of broad O–H stretch (~3200–3600 cm⁻¹).
- Concept: acidity of phenols, Williamson ether synthesis (SN2), IR monitoring.

#### 7.79 — Bromotriphenylmethane solvolysis, fn 3, J. Chem. Ed. 2009, 86, 853–855
- SM 1: Ph3C–Br `BrC(c1ccccc1)(c1ccccc1)c1ccccc1`; ROH → Ph3C–OR: 2a R = H (triphenylmethanol), 2b R = CH3, 2c R = CH2CH3.
- Asks: (a) mechanism 1 → 2b; (b) all three conversions are almost instantaneous — draw structure that justifies this; (c) why IR is ideal to follow 1 → 2a but other spectroscopy is better for 1 → 2b / 2c.
- Answer [H]: SN1 (solvolysis): ionization to trityl cation (highly resonance-stabilized over three rings — the structure to draw), attack by MeOH, deprotonation. IR: 2a shows new O–H stretch; 2b/2c ethers have no diagnostic new IR band (C–O only) → NMR better (OCH3 singlet / OCH2CH3 quartet-triplet).

#### 7.80 — (±)-camerodnanol (from *Echinops giganteus* flower essential oil), fn 4, Org. Lett. 2000, 2, 2717–2719 (도전 문제, p.339)
- SM: bicyclo[3.3.0]octanone (diquinane) bearing gem-dimethyl next to ketone, angular methyl at ring fusion, and a side chain CH(CH3)–CH2CH2Br on the carbon adjacent to the α-ring-fusion CH; exact SMILES uncertain.
- Reagent: KOt-Bu → angular triquinane ketone (new cyclopentane ring; the ketone α-carbon at the ring fusion becomes quaternary).
- Asks: plausible mechanism.
- Answer [H]: t-BuO⁻ deprotonates the α-C–H (ring-fusion carbon α to C=O; the other α-carbon is quaternary gem-dimethyl so no competition) → enolate; intramolecular SN2 (C-alkylation) on the primary C–Br → 5-membered ring, Br⁻ leaves.
- Concept: enolate as nucleophile, intramolecular SN2 forming 5-ring.

#### 7.81 — thienamycin (from *Streptomyces cattleya*), fn 5, J. Am. Chem. Soc. 1980, 102, 6161–6163
- SM 1: (S)-4-(iodomethyl)-1-(trimethylsilyl)azetidin-2-one (CH2I on wedge) `C[Si](C)(C)N1C(=O)C[C@H]1CI` [M on stereo label]. Nucleophile 2: 2-lithio-2-(trimethylsilyl)-1,3-dithiane `[Li]C1([Si](C)(C)C)SCCCS1`.
- SN2 → 3. Asks: (a) draw 3; (b) why the dithiane ring in 3 exists as two chair conformers in comparable amounts while in 2 it is essentially one.
- Answer: (a) [H] 3 = 4-{[2-(trimethylsilyl)-1,3-dithian-2-yl]methyl}-1-TMS-azetidin-2-one `C[Si](C)(C)N1C(=O)C[C@H]1CC1([Si](C)(C)C)SCCCS1` (C14H29NOS2Si2; configuration retained—substitution at CH2, not the stereocentre). (b) [M] In 2, C2 bears SiMe3 vs Li (small) → SiMe3 strongly prefers equatorial, one chair; in 3, C2 bears two large groups (SiMe3 and CH2-azetidinone) of similar size → either chair puts one bulky group axial; two conformers of similar energy.

#### 7.82 — 2-octyl sulfonate hydrolysis in water/dioxane, fn 6, J. Am. Chem. Soc. 1965, 87, 287–291
- SM: optically pure (R)-2-octyl sulfonate (ROSO2-O-CH(CH3)C6H13); H2O/dioxane → (S)-2-octanol `CCCCCC[C@H](C)O` with optical purity 77% (25:75 water:dioxane), 88% (50:50), 95% (75:25), 100% (100:0).
- Asks: complete mechanism for formation of (R)-2-octanol in the presence of dioxane.
- Answer [H]: two successive SN2: dioxane O attacks C2 (inversion) → oxonium (dioxanyl-alkyl cation); water then attacks C2 displacing dioxane (second inversion) → net retention → (R)-2-octanol, then deprotonation. Direct SN2 by water gives (S).

#### 7.83 — cyclopropanol synthesis, fn 7, Tetrahedron Lett. 1967, 8, 4941–4944
- 1 cyclopropyl chloride ✗(NaOH)→ 4 cyclopropanol. Route: 1 + Mg → 2 cyclopropylmagnesium chloride; 2 + tert-butyl peroxyacetate (t-BuO–O–C(O)CH3) → 3 cyclopropyl tert-butyl ether `CC(C)(C)OC1CC1`; H3O⁺ → 4 `OC1CC1`.
- Asks: (a) mechanism 2→3 (SN2-type on O) and identify LG; (b) why irreversible; (c) mechanisms 3→4 via SN1 and via SN2; (d) the other alcohol by-product.
- Answer [H]: (a) carbanion attacks the t-BuO oxygen of the O–O bond; LG = acetate (CH3CO2⁻). (b) acetate is a far weaker base/nucleophile than the cyclopropyl carbanion (pKa ~4.8 vs ~46) → equilibrium hugely favours products. (c) protonate ether O. SN1: C(t-Bu)–O cleavage releases cyclopropanol and the tert-butyl cation; water traps it, deprotonation → t-BuOH. "SN2" alternative: water attacks (concerted) the carbon of the protonated ether with cyclopropanol as LG (book wants the cleavage on the t-Bu side so cyclopropanol is released intact; SN1 is chemically the realistic path). (d) tert-butanol (CH3)3COH [H].

#### 7.84 — 1-deoxynojirimycin (HIV chemotherapy agent) synthesis, fn 8, Org. Lett. 2010, 12, 136–139
- SM 1: N-benzyl azepane (7-ring) with fused epoxide (3,4-epoxy) bearing OR and adjacent OMs (mesylate, wedge); Cl⁻ → 2: N-benzyl piperidine (fused epoxide) with exocyclic CH2Cl on the carbon adjacent to N (ring contraction). SMILES uncertain.
- Asks: plausible mechanism with stereochemical evidence.
- Answer [H]: neighbouring-group participation: ring N attacks the C–OMs carbon intramolecularly (SN2, inversion) → bicyclic aziridinium ion; Cl⁻ opens aziridinium at the former ring CH2 (less hindered, SN2) → piperidine + CH2Cl; net double inversion at the carbinol carbon → retention-type outcome consistent with drawn stereochemistry.

#### 7.85 — solvolysis of toluene halogen derivatives (PhCXYZ), fn 9, J. Am. Chem. Soc. 1951, 73, 22–23
- Hydrolysis (SN1) in 50% aqueous acetone, 30 °C; rates (×10⁴ min⁻¹): X=H,Y=Cl: Z=H 0.22, Z=Cl 2.21, Z=Br 31.1; X=Cl,Y=Cl: Z=H 2.21, Cl 110.5, Br 2122; X=Br,Y=Br: Z=H 6.85, Cl 1803, Br 1131.
- Asks: (a) which is better LG, Cl⁻ or Br⁻ (use Fig. 7.28), support with data; (b) is a carbocation stabilized by adjacent Cl (resonance structures); (c) by adjacent Br; (d) Cl vs Br which stabilizes more; (e) which factor matters more, LG ability or cation stability.
- Table decoded: row X=H,Y=Cl: PhCH2Cl 0.22 / PhCHCl2 2.21 / PhCHClBr 31.1; row X=Y=Cl: PhCHCl2 2.21 / PhCCl3 110.5 / PhCCl2Br 2122; row X=Y=Br: PhCHBr2 6.85 / PhCBr2Cl 1803 / PhCBr3 1131.
- Answer [M]: (a) Br⁻ is the better LG: same cation, different LG — PhCHCl2 (2.21) vs PhCHClBr (31.1); PhCCl3 (110.5) vs PhCCl2Br (2122). (b) α-Cl stabilizes the cation by lone-pair donation (C=Cl⁺ resonance): PhCH2Cl 0.22 → PhCHCl2 2.21 → PhCCl3 110.5 (same LG Cl). (c) α-Br also stabilizes: PhCHBr2 6.85 and PhCBr2Cl 1803 ≫ PhCH2Cl/PhCH2-type. (d) Data are mixed: with Cl leaving, PhCBr2⁺ formation (PhCBr2Cl, 1803) is faster than PhCCl2⁺ (PhCCl3, 110.5), but with Br leaving PhCCl2Br (2122) > PhCBr3 (1131). Textbook expected answer likely: Cl stabilizes better (2p–3p overlap) — low confidence; check original paper. (e) Most likely "leaving-group ability" dominates in the H-series, cation stabilization dominates overall (rate spans 10⁴ with α-halogens) — [L].

#### 7.86 — vinylic SN2 forming 2-alkylidene aziridine, fn 10, J. Am. Chem. Soc. 2004, 126, 6868–6869
- SM 1: (E)-2-bromobut-2-en-1-ol `OC/C(Br)=C\C` (CH2OH cis to CH3) [M on geometry]. 1) TsCl, pyridine → 2; 2) BnNH2 → 3; 3) NaNH2, NH3, −78 °C → 4: 1-benzyl-2-ethylideneaziridine `C/C=C1/CN1Cc1ccccc1` (CH3 cis to N, as drawn) [M].
- Asks: (a) structures 2, 3; (b) mechanism/TS for 3→4 (NaNH2 base); (c) how stereochemistry matches SN2-type process.
- Answer: 2 = allylic tosylate `CC=C(Br)COS(=O)(=O)c1ccc(C)cc1` (E); 3 = N-benzyl-2-bromobut-2-en-1-amine (SN2 at sp3 allylic C; vinylic C–Br untouched) [H]. NaNH2 deprotonates N–H → amide anion attacks the vinylic C–Br carbon in-plane from the back side (σ* C–Br), Br⁻ leaves → 3-ring (in-plane SNV σ). Alkene geometry inverted: Br was trans to CH3, N ends up cis to CH3 [M].

#### 7.87 — aziridine → azetidine ring expansion, fn 11, J. Org. Chem. 2012, 77, 3181–3190
- SM: 1-(arylmethyl)-2-(bromomethyl)-2-methylaziridine (R = CH2Ar) `CC1(CBr)CN1Cc1ccccc1` (aryl generic); NaOAc, CH3CN → minor: (1-R-2-methylaziridin-2-yl)methyl acetate; major: 1-R-3-methylazetidin-3-yl acetate `CC(=O)OC1(C)CN(Cc2ccccc2)C1` (Ar=Ph example).
- Asks: mechanism explaining both products (hint: chapter's medicinal chemistry – mustard/aziridinium).
- Answer [H]: aziridine N displaces Br⁻ intramolecularly → bicyclic 1-azoniabicyclo[1.1.0]butane ion; acetate opens it: attack at the quaternary bridgehead C (cleaving the more strained central bond, SN1-like/SN2) → azetidine (major); attack at the CH2 → regenerates aziridine with OAc (minor). (Minor product may also arise by direct SN2 on CH2Br.)

#### 7.88 — biotin (Hoffmann-La Roche total synthesis), fn 12, J. Am. Chem. Soc. 1982, 104, 6460–6462
- SM 1: biotin precursor: cis-fused tetrahydrothieno[3,4-d]imidazol-2-one with side chain CH(OH)–(CH2)3–COOH (OH wedge) at C next to S. SOCl2, pyridine → [2: acid chloride + chlorosulfite ROS(O)Cl] → 3 (acyl chloride + secondary alkyl chloride, retention by X-ray) → 4 biotin `OC(=O)CCCC[C@@H]1SC[C@@H]2NC(=O)N[C@H]12` (biotin SMILES standard [H]; precursor stereo uncertain).
- Asks: propose mechanism explaining overall retention of configuration in 2 → 3 (hint: Section 7.9 medicinal chemistry = sulfur mustards).
- Answer [H]: neighbouring-group participation by ring sulfur: S attacks the carbon bearing OS(O)Cl (intramolecular SN2, inversion; SO2 + Cl⁻ released) → bicyclic sulfonium ion; Cl⁻ then attacks that carbon (SN2, second inversion) opening the sulfonium → net retention.

## Chapter 8 — 알켄: 구조 및 제거 반응을 이용한 제법 (Alkenes: Structure and Preparation via Elimination Reactions)  (book pp. 395–404; PDF p-10 … p-19)

### Key reactions / concepts from summary (p-10–p-14)
- **E1 dehydration of alcohols**: conc. H2SO4, heat → most substituted (Zaitsev) alkene (e.g. 1,2-dimethylcyclohexanol → 1,2-dimethylcyclohexene); carbocation, possible rearrangement; regioselective, stereoselective (trans), not stereospecific.
- **E2 dehydrohalogenation, Zaitsev product**: NaOEt (unhindered strong base) → more substituted alkene.
- **E2 dehydrohalogenation, Hofmann product**: t-BuOK (bulky base) → less substituted alkene.
- E2: concerted, rate = k[substrate][base], 3° fastest; requires anti-periplanar H/LG (on cyclohexane both axial); stereospecific when β-carbon has one H.
- E1: rate = k[substrate]; competes with SN1. Alkene naming, E/Z, Bredt's rule, stability vs substitution.
- Reagent roles: Nu only (halides, HS⁻, RS⁻, H2S, RSH); base only (H⁻, DBN, DBU); strong Nu/strong base (HO⁻, MeO⁻, EtO⁻, t-BuO⁻ — t-BuO⁻ mostly base); weak Nu/weak base (H2O, MeOH, EtOH). 1° + strong base → SN2 major (t-BuOK → E2), 2° → E2 major, 3° → E2 only.
- 8.14 retrosynthesis example: ethyl acetate ⇐ NaOAc + EtBr (SN2).
- Note: 8.53 Dactylyne (marine natural product, E/Z assignment) has NO footnote.

#### 8.89 — Pladienolide B (macrolide from *Streptomyces platensis* mutant strain), fn 1, Org. Lett. 2012, 14, 4730–4733 (종합 문제, p.402)
- 31-step stereoselective synthesis reported in 2012 (vs earlier 59-step). Structure only (no reaction).
- Constitution SMILES (no stereo) [H for constitution, checked C30H48O8]: `CCC(O)C(C)C1OC1CC(C)C=CC=C(C)C2OC(=O)CC(O)CCC(C)(O)C(OC(C)=O)C=CC2C` — 12-membered lactone with 3-OH, 6-OH/6-Me (tertiary), 7-OAc, 8,9-ring E-alkene, 10-Me, side chain with trisubstituted C12=C13(Me) and C14=C15 diene, 16-Me, 18,19-epoxide, 20-Me, 21-OH.
- Asks: (a) identify trisubstituted π bond and its E/Z; (b) number of possible stereoisomers; (c) draw enantiomer; (d) diastereomer differing only at chiral centres bonded directly to ester oxygens (C7 bearing OAc and C11 lactone carbon); (e) diastereomer differing only in configuration of the disubstituted π bond outside the ring (C14=C15).
- Answers: (a) C12=C13 (bearing CH3), E [M — natural pladienolide B is (8E,12E,14E)]. (b) 10 chirality centres + 3 stereogenic C=C = 13 → 2¹³ = 8192 [H on counting from drawn structure]. (c) invert all centres, keep alkene geometry. (d) flip C7 and C11. (e) make C14=C15 Z.

#### 8.90 — steroid derivative (cholic-acid-type), fn 2, Tetrahedron Lett. 2010, 51, 6948–6950 (도전 문제)
- SM 1a: methyl 3,7-diacetoxy-12-hydroxy-cholanoate (5β-steroid; 3-OAc, 7-OAc (axial, down), 12-OH axial down; C11 substituents R (equatorial, drawn toward C10-Me) and R' (axial, up/β); side chain CH(CH3)CH2CH2CO2CH3). 1a R=R'=H; 1b R=H, R'=D; 1c R=D, R'=H. SMILES uncertain (likely methyl 3α,7α-diacetoxy-12α-hydroxy-5β-cholan-24-oate).
- Conditions: TsCl, pyridine; then NaOAc (as base) → 2a.
- Asks: (a) structure of 2a; (b) which of 2b/2c retains D.
- Answer: (a) 12-OTs then E2 → Δ11 alkene (C11=C12; C13 is quaternary so only C11 H's available): methyl 3,7-diacetoxy-5β-chol-11-enoate [H]. (b) E2 requires anti-periplanar (trans-diaxial) H; 12α-OTs is axial (down) so the 11β axial H (R') is removed. 1b (R'=D) loses D → 2b has no D; 1c (R=D, equatorial) keeps D → **2c contains deuterium** [M-H].

#### 8.91 — oxetane-containing diastereomers (oxetane as in taxol), fn 3, Tetrahedron Lett. 2007, 48, 8353–8355
- SM 1: Ph-CH2CH2-CH(OH)-(oxetan-2-yl), OH hashed, oxetane O hashed; SM 2: same with OH wedged (diastereomer). Constitution `OC(CCc1ccccc1)C1CCO1`.
- Conditions: TsCl, pyridine; then t-BuOK → Zaitsev product predominates despite bulky base.
- Asks: stereochemical outcome for each, draw products, justify with Newman projections.
- Answer [M]: tosylation (retention), anti-periplanar E2 removing the single oxetane C2–H → trisubstituted exocyclic enol-ether alkene 2-(3-phenylpropylidene)oxetane `C(=C1CCO1)CCc1ccccc1`. Because the β-carbon has only one H, E2 is stereospecific: 1 and 2 give opposite geometric isomers. By my Newman analysis of the drawn configurations: 1 → (Z) (CH2CH2Ph cis to ring O), 2 → (E). Geometry assignment medium confidence.

#### 8.92 — debromination of 1,2-dibromo-1,2-diphenylethane in DMF, fn 4, J. Org. Chem. 1991, 56, 2582–2584
- SM 1: meso-1,2-dibromo-1,2-diphenylethane (as drawn, anti-periplanar Br/Br gives E) `Br[C@@H](c1ccccc1)[C@H](Br)c1ccccc1` (meso, 1R,2S) [M]; DMF → only (E)-stilbene 2 `C(=C/c1ccccc1)\c1ccccc1`. Proposed concerted mechanism: DMF carbonyl O attacks Br (anti-periplanar Br–C–C–Br, E2-like).
- Asks: (a) why proposed concerted mechanism conflicts with observations; (b) stereoselective or stereospecific?
- Answer [H]: concerted anti elimination from the other diastereomer (dl / (R,R)/(S,S)) would have to give (Z)-stilbene, but it also gives only E → mechanism is stepwise (e.g. loss of Br⁻/carbocation or bromonium-type intermediate with free rotation, then loss of Br⁺ to DMF) → product determined by stability (trans). (b) stereoselective, not stereospecific.

#### 8.93 — (+)-coronafacic acid (component of phytotoxin coronatine), fn 5, J. Org. Chem. 2009, 74, 2433–2437
- SM: cis-hydrindane: cyclopentane ring fused, bearing spiro 5,5-dimethyl-1,3-dioxane ketal at C1 (ketone protected); six-membered ring carries ethyl (wedge), adjacent secondary OH (hashed), adjacent CH–CO2CH3 (CO2Me hashed). SMILES uncertain.
- Conditions: 1) TsCl, pyridine; 2) DBU → ?
- Answer [M-H]: OTs formed with retention; DBU E2 removes H α to the ester (anti-periplanar: OTs and CO2Me both hashed → α-H up, trans/anti-diaxial possible; on the ethyl-bearing carbon the H is cis to OTs, cannot be anti-periplanar) → α,β-unsaturated methyl ester (C=C between ester carbon and former carbinol carbon, conjugated with C=O). Regiochemistry justified by anti-periplanar requirement + acidity/conjugation.

#### 8.94 — intramolecular Schmidt-type reaction of azido ketal, fn 6, J. Am. Chem. Soc. 2010, 132, 2530–2531
- SM 1: 1,4-dioxaspiro[4.5]decane (ethylene ketal of cyclohexanone) with C6 quaternary (R + CH2CH2CH2N3) and C8-R. Under acid → intermediate 2 (bicyclic aminodiazonium: azide N attached to the former ketal carbon, which bears O–CH2CH2OH ether; N–N2⁺). Steps x,y,z to product 3.
- Asks: draw mechanism for last three steps as described: (x) alkyl group anti-periplanar to N2⁺ undergoes 1,2-shift to N with N2 loss (backside displacement); (y) alcohol O attacks resulting carbocation (oxocarbenium / iminium-type carbon); (z) oxonium deprotonated.
- Answer [M]: 1,2-C→N migration (ring expansion, Schmidt) gives a carbocation at the former ketal carbon stabilized by O (and N) → pendant 2-hydroxyethoxy OH closes back to a 1,3-dioxolane → after deprotonation 3 = bridged/fused bicyclic amine (ring-expanded azacycle) bearing a 2,2-(ethylenedioxy) N,O,O-orthoamide carbon. Exact structure of 3 uncertain (depends on which C–C bond is anti to N2⁺; the analogy is E2 anti-periplanar).

#### 8.95 — base strength vs. regioselectivity (2-iodobutane), fn 7, J. Am. Chem. Soc. 1973, 95, 3405–3407
- 2-iodobutane, DMSO, 50 °C, oxyanion bases → % 1-butene of total butenes: potassium benzoate 7.2; potassium phenoxide 11.4; sodium 2,2,2-trifluoroethoxide 14.3; sodium ethoxide 17.1.
- Asks: (a) trend vs base strength and relation between reactivity and selectivity; (b) predict % 1-butene with potassium 4-nitrophenoxide (pKa 4-nitrophenol 7.1).
- Answer: (a) [H] conjugate-acid pKa: benzoic ~4.2 < 4-nitrophenol 7.1 < phenol ~10 < CF3CH2OH ~12.4 < EtOH ~16; stronger base → more Hofmann (1-butene), i.e. more reactive base is less selective (earlier, more carbanion-like TS / less alkene character). (b) [M] between benzoate and phenoxide: ≈ 9% (8–10%).

#### 8.96 — 2-butyl-Y + t-BuOK/t-BuOH, 50 °C, fn 8, J. Am. Chem. Soc. 1965, 87, 5517–5518
- trans:cis 2-butene ratio: Y = I 2.2, Br 1.4, Cl 1.3, OTs 0.6 (1-butene major in all).
- Asks: transition-state argument with Newman projections for why tosylate favours cis.
- Answer [L–M]: In anti-periplanar Newman projections along C2–C3, the C3-methyl is gauche to the LG in both 2-butene TSs; trans TS places the C3-CH3 between LG and C2–H, cis TS places it between LG and C2–CH3. For halides, CH3/CH3 gauche strain dominates → trans. For the large, oxygen-bonded tosylate (O–SO2Ar rotates over C2–H side / the bulky Ts group), the dominant repulsion is between the C3-methyl and the tosyl group, which is larger in the trans-forming conformer where the methyl sits next to the region occupied by the sulfonyl group → cis favoured. (Also bulky base t-BuO⁻ interacts with the C2 substituent flanking the H removed.) Confidence low — verify against the paper.

---

## Chapter 9 — 알켄의 첨가 반응 (Addition Reactions of Alkenes)  (book pp. 452–461; PDF p-20 … p-29)
Note: chapter 9's end-of-chapter problems run past p-26 (to p-28) and its 참고문헌 is on p-29; all footnoted ch. 9 problems are covered here (overlaps possible with whoever extracts p-27+). Chapter 10 (알카인, book p.489) starts on p-30 — not covered here.

### Key reactions from summary (반응의 복습 wheel, p-20, shown on 1-methylcyclohexene)
1. HX → Markovnikov hydrohalogenation (3° halide); carbocation, possible rearrangement.
2. HBr, ROOR → anti-Markovnikov hydrobromination (radical).
3. H3O⁺ → acid-catalyzed hydration (Markovnikov, carbocation, rearrangement possible); 1) Hg(OAc)2, H2O 2) NaBH4 → oxymercuration–demercuration (Markovnikov, no rearrangement, mercurinium ion).
4. 1) BH3·THF 2) H2O2, NaOH → hydroboration–oxidation (anti-Markovnikov, syn addition; trans-2-methylcyclohexanol + En).
5. H2, Pt → catalytic hydrogenation (syn addition).
6. Br2 → bromination via bromonium ion, anti addition (trans-1,2-dibromide + En).
7. Br2, H2O → halohydrin (OH at more substituted C, anti).
8. 1) RCO3H 2) H3O⁺ → anti dihydroxylation (via epoxide).
9. 1) OsO4 2) NaHSO3, H2O, or KMnO4/NaOH cold → syn dihydroxylation.
10. 1) O3 2) DMS → ozonolysis (6-oxoheptanal from 1-methylcyclohexene).
- Also: asymmetric hydrogenation with chiral catalysts; changing LG position (t-BuOK then HBr/ROOR) and π-bond position (HBr then t-BuOK).

### Literature-based problems (참고문헌 on p-29, book p. 461)
