## Chapter 21 — 카복실산과 그 유도체 (Carboxylic Acids and Their Derivatives)
PDF pp. 11–20 = book pp. 1004–1013.

### Key reactions (반응의 복습, book pp. 1004–1005)
- Preparation of RCOOH: RCH2Br → NaCN → H3O+/heat; RBr → Mg, CO2, H3O+. Reaction: RCOOH → RCH2OH (xs LiAlH4 or BH3·THF).
- Acid chlorides (from RCOOH + SOCl2): + ROH/py → ester; + xs NH3 / RNH2 / R2NH → amides; + H2O/py → acid; + xs LiAlH4 → 1° alcohol; + LiAl(OtBu)3H → aldehyde; + xs RMgBr → 3° alcohol; + R2CuLi → ketone.
- Anhydrides: analogous (acid + heat, or NaOH then CH3COCl).
- Esters: prep by RCOOH + NaOH then CH3I; Fischer ([H+], MeOH); RCOCl + ROH/py. Reactions: H3O+ or NaOH/heat (saponification) → acid; NH3 → amide; xs LiAlH4 → alcohol; DIBAH → aldehyde; xs RMgBr → 3° alcohol.
- Amides: RCOCl + 2 NH3; hydrolysis (H3O+ or NaOH/heat); xs LiAlH4 → amine; SOCl2 → nitrile.
- Nitriles: RBr + NaCN; amide + SOCl2; hydrolysis → RCOOH; RMgBr then H3O+ → ketone; xs LiAlH4 → RCH2NH2.
Summary concepts: nucleophilic acyl substitution (tetrahedral intermediate; no H⁻/C⁻ leaving groups), reactivity order acid halide > anhydride > ester > amide; Henderson–Hasselbalch (carboxylates at physiological pH); IR C=O 1650–1850; ¹³C 160–185 (nitrile 115–130); COOH ¹H ~12 ppm.

### References (참고문헌, book p. 1013 / PDF p. 20)
1. Org. Lett. 2000, 2, 791–794.
2. J. Org. Chem. 2012, 77, 5503–5514.
3. Tetrahedron Lett. 2012, 53, 1859–1862.
4. Org. Biomol. Chem. 2006, 4, 2119–2157.
5. Tetrahedron Lett. 2010, 51, 6542–6544.
6. J. Am. Chem. Soc. 1961, 83, 4214–4216.
7. J. Am. Chem. Soc. 1970, 92, 397–398.
8. J. Org. Chem. 1984, 49, 4094–4095.
9. Tetrahedron Lett. 2008, 49, 6853–6855.
10. Angew. Chem. Int. Ed. 2009, 48, 9105–9108.

### Literature problems

#### 21.81 — book p. 1012 (PDF p. 19) — footnote 1 — Org. Lett. 2000, 2, 791–794
- Context: olefin metathesis (Grubbs; 2005 Nobel) — RCM to 2,6-disubstituted 3,6-dihydro-2H-pyran.
- SM 1: 1-phenylbut-3-en-1-ol (single enantiomer, Ph wedge) `OC(CC=C)c1ccccc1`.
- Steps: 1) NaH, then ethyl 2-bromopropanoate (CH3CHBrCO2Et, drawn with defined stereocentre); 2) DIBAH; 3) Ph3P=CH2 → diene ether 2; then Grubbs catalyst → 3.
- Asked: product of each step.
- Expected answer [H]: step 1: alkoxide SN2 (inversion at the bromide carbon) → ethyl 2-(1-phenylbut-3-enyloxy)propanoate `CCOC(=O)C(C)OC(CC=C)c1ccccc1`; step 2: DIBAH (1 equiv, low T) → aldehyde `O=CC(C)OC(CC=C)c1ccccc1`; step 3: Wittig → 2 `C=CC(C)OC(CC=C)c1ccccc1` (C14H18O); RCM → 3, 2-methyl-6-phenyl-3,6-dihydro-2H-pyran `CC1OC(c2ccccc2)CC=C1` + ethylene. Stereo: 2,6-cis/trans as drawn (both wedge-type, from inversion of bromide).

#### 21.82 — book p. 1012 (PDF p. 19) — footnote 2 — J. Org. Chem. 2012, 77, 5503–5514
- Context: aminotetralins (antidepressant candidates).
- SM: 1-bromo-3,5-dimethylbenzene `Cc1cc(C)cc(Br)c1` → (3-bromo-5-methylphenyl)acetic acid `Cc1cc(Br)cc(CC(=O)O)c1` in 3 steps.
- Expected answer [H]: (1) NBS, hν (or heat/peroxide) — benzylic monobromination; (2) NaCN (SN2) → ArCH2CN; (3) H3O+, heat → ArCH2COOH.

#### 21.83 — book p. 1012 (PDF p. 19) — footnote 3 — Tetrahedron Lett. 2012, 53, 1859–1862
- Context: steroid derivatives; intermediate for pentacyclic steroids.
- SM 1: cholic-acid-derived methyl ester: 3α,7α-dimethoxy-12α-hydroxy-5β-cholan-24-oate methyl ester (12α-OH, C24 CO2Me). Product 2: 12α-O-allyl ether; side chain C24 reduced to CH2–O–C(=O)CH=CH2 (acrylate ester); 3α,7α-OMe retained.
- Asked: plausible three-step synthesis.
- Expected answer [M-H]: (1) NaH, then CH2=CHCH2Br (Williamson allylation of 12-OH); (2) xs LiAlH4, then H2O (ester → 1° alcohol C24-OH); (3) CH2=CHCOCl (acryloyl chloride), pyridine → acrylate ester. (Order matters: allylate before reduction so only one OH is free at each stage.)

#### 21.84 — book p. 1012 (PDF p. 19) — footnote 4 — Org. Biomol. Chem. 2006, 4, 2119–2157
- Context: key fragment for total synthesis of an analog of **amphidinolide N** (cytotoxic, marine dinoflagellate Amphidinium sp.).
- SM 1: (E)-α,β-unsaturated (2-methyl-2-enoic) carboxylic acid bearing stereocentres with Me, OPMB (p-methoxybenzyl ether), OTBS (OSiMe2tBu) and a TMS-capped alkyne (C≡C–SiMe3). Product 2: the corresponding allylic bromide (CO2H → CH2Br).
- Expected answer [M]: (1) LiAlH4 (or esterify then DIBAH ×2), then H2O → allylic 1° alcohol; (2) PBr3 (or CBr4/PPh3, or MsCl then LiBr) → allylic bromide. Protecting groups untouched.

#### 21.85 — book p. 1012 (PDF p. 19) — footnote 5 — Tetrahedron Lett. 2010, 51, 6542–6544
- Context: intermediate 3 for γ-secretase inhibitor **BMS-708163**.
- SM 1: 4-(bromomethyl)-3-fluorobenzonitrile `N#Cc1ccc(CBr)c(F)c1`; KOAc (CH3CO2K), DMF → 2, 4-(acetoxymethyl)-3-fluorobenzonitrile `CC(=O)OCc1ccc(C#N)cc1F`; NaOMe, MeOH → 3, 3-fluoro-4-(hydroxymethyl)benzonitrile `N#Cc1ccc(CO)c(F)c1`.
- Asked: (a) why NaOH(aq) cannot convert 1 → 3 directly (side products); (b) full mechanism of the two steps.
- Expected answer: (a) [M] hydroxide also hydrolyses the nitrile (→ amide/carboxylate: 3-fluoro-4-(hydroxymethyl)benzamide / benzoic acid); also possible dibenzyl ether (alkoxide of 3 + 1) and SNAr of F. (b) [H] SN2 of acetate on benzylic bromide (one step); then transesterification: MeO⁻ adds to ester C=O → tetrahedral intermediate → expels ArCH2O⁻ → proton transfer from MeOH gives 3 + MeOAc (catalytic methoxide regenerated).

#### 21.86 — book p. 1012 (PDF p. 19) — footnote 6 — J. Am. Chem. Soc. 1961, 83, 4214–4216
- Reaction: m-/p-substituted methyl benzoates + NaOH, dioxane:H2O (3:2), 35 °C → sodium benzoates + CH3OH. Rate constants k (M⁻¹min⁻¹): p-NO2 102; m-NO2 63; m-Cl 9.1; m-Br 8.6; H 1.7; p-CH3 0.98; p-OCH3 0.42; p-NH2 0.06.
- Asked: structural explanation of the trend, with resonance structures.
- Expected answer [H]: rate-determining hydroxide addition builds negative charge; EWGs (p-NO2: resonance + induction, largest; m-NO2: induction only; m-Cl/m-Br: induction) increase C=O electrophilicity → faster. EDGs (p-CH3 hyperconjugation/induction; p-OCH3 and especially p-NH2 resonance donation into the ring/carbonyl, drawn as C=O⁻ resonance forms) reduce electrophilicity → slower. (Hammett-type correlation.)

#### 21.87 — book p. 1013 (PDF p. 20) — footnote 7 — J. Am. Chem. Soc. 1970, 92, 397–398
- Context: E. J. Corey prostaglandin synthesis.
- SM 1: 7-(methoxymethyl)bicyclo[2.2.1]hept-5-en-2-one. → 2 → 3: cis-2-[5-hydroxy-4-(methoxymethyl)cyclopent-2-enyl]acetic acid (hydroxy acid; relative stereo as drawn).
- Asked: reagents for 1 → 2 → 3; structure of 2.
- Expected answer [H]: 1 → 2: peroxyacid (MCPBA, NaHCO3) Baeyer–Villiger; O inserts between C=O and the bridgehead (more substituted, migrates) → bicyclic δ-lactone 2 (2-oxabicyclo[3.2.1]oct-6-en-3-one bearing CH2OMe on C8). 2 → 3: NaOH(aq) hydrolysis, then H3O+ → hydroxy acid 3.

#### 21.88 — book p. 1013 (PDF p. 20) — footnote 8 — J. Org. Chem. 1984, 49, 4094–4095
- Context: cornerstone two-step rearrangement in stereoselective total synthesis of **quadrone** (bioactive, Aspergillus fungus).
- SM: tricyclic (bridged) carbocycle with secondary mesylate (OMs) and CO2H; gem-dimethyl on bridge. Reagents: 1) NaH; 2) H2SO4, H2O. Product: rearranged tetracyclic skeleton with γ/δ-lactone whose ring O is on a quaternary (ring-fusion) carbon.
- Asked: mechanism.
- Expected answer [L-M]: (1) NaH deprotonates CO2H; ionization of the mesylate is assisted by the antiperiplanar C–C bond (Wagner–Meerwein 1,2-alkyl shift, skeletal rearrangement) and loss of H⁺ gives a rearranged alkene; (2) H2SO4 protonates the alkene (Markovnikov) → tertiary carbocation at the ring-fusion carbon; intramolecular capture by the CO2H oxygen and deprotonation → lactone. Exact bonds should be checked against the 1984 paper (structure drawn with heavy-line perspective).

#### 21.89 — book p. 1013 (PDF p. 20) — footnote 9 — Tetrahedron Lett. 2008, 49, 6853–6855
- Context: determination of absolute configuration of a bromohydrin; unexpected skeletal rearrangement.
- SM 1: cis-fused cyclopenta[b]furan-2-one (hexahydro, γ-lactone) bearing ring-OH (α to the CH2-side ring fusion carbon) and Br (on the carbon adjacent to the O-bearing ring fusion carbon), trans to the lactone O. NaH → 2: isomeric fused γ-lactone (lactone O now on the former C–OH carbon) with an epoxide on the two carbons that bore the former lactone O and Br.
- Expected answer [M-H]: NaH → alkoxide; intramolecular acyl transfer (translactonization): alkoxide attacks lactone C=O → tetrahedral intermediate → expels the original ring-fusion alkoxide → new γ-lactone; liberated alkoxide is anti to adjacent C–Br → intramolecular SN2 (Williamson) → epoxide 2.

#### 21.90 — book p. 1013 (PDF p. 20) — footnote 10 — Angew. Chem. Int. Ed. 2009, 48, 9105–9108
- Context: total synthesis of **englerin A** (cytotoxic; stem bark of a Tanzanian plant); unusual base-catalysed ring contraction.
- SM: cyclopentane-fused δ-lactone in which the alkyl O-carbon is part of an epoxide (spiro/fused epoxy-lactone: O–CH(epoxide)–C(Me)(epoxide)); NaOMe (cat.) → cyclopentane-fused γ-lactone with a quaternary carbon bearing CH3 and CHO.
- Expected answer [H]: MeO⁻ adds to lactone C=O → tetrahedral intermediate → ring opens to methyl ester + alkoxide on the epoxide CH carbon; that alkoxide collapses to C=O (aldehyde) breaking the epoxide C–O bond → tertiary alkoxide on C(Me); this alkoxide attacks the methyl ester (nucleophilic acyl substitution) → γ-lactone, releasing MeO⁻ (catalytic).

## Chapter 22 — 알파 탄소 화학: 엔올과 엔올 음이온 (Alpha-Carbon Chemistry: Enols and Enolates)
PDF pp. 21–31 = book pp. 1057–1067.

### Key reactions (반응의 복습, book pp. 1057–1058)
- α-Halogenation: ketone + Br2, [H3O+] → α-bromoketone + HBr (acid-catalysed, autocatalytic); Hell–Volhard–Zelinsky (Br2, PBr3; H2O) → α-bromo acid; haloform (NaOH, Br2; H3O+) methyl ketone → acid.
- Aldol addition (NaOH, H2O → β-hydroxy aldehyde) and aldol condensation (heat → α,β-unsaturated, E1cb); crossed aldol (HCHO + propanal); intramolecular aldol (hexane-2,5-dione → 3-methylcyclopent-2-enone).
- Claisen condensation (NaOEt; H3O+ → β-keto ester); crossed Claisen (ethyl benzoate + ethyl acetate); Dieckmann (diethyl adipate → 2-carbethoxycyclopentanone).
- Alkylation via enolates: LDA, −78 °C (kinetic, less substituted side) vs NaH, 25 °C (thermodynamic, more substituted side).
- Malonic ester synthesis (NaOEt; RBr; H3O+, heat → RCH2COOH); acetoacetic ester synthesis (→ methyl ketone).
- Michael addition: R2CuLi to enals; stabilized nucleophiles (1,3-dione + KOH + enal); Stork enamine (R2NH/[H+]; MVK; H3O+ → 1,5-dicarbonyl); Robinson annulation (2-methylcyclohexane-1,3-dione + MVK, NaOH, heat → Wieland–Miescher-type enone).
Summary concepts: keto–enol tautomerism; enolate formation; retro-aldol; 5-/6-membered ring preference; decarboxylation of β-keto acids; 1,3- vs 1,5-difunctional relationships; tandem conjugate addition–alkylation (α,β-dialkylation).

### References (참고문헌, book p. 1067 / PDF p. 31)
1. J. Am. Chem. Soc. 1954, 76, 2029–2030.
2. J. Org. Chem. 1977, 42, 1663–1664.
3. J. Org. Chem. 2012, 77, 6371–6376.
4. J. Am. Chem. Soc. 2012, 134, 9291–9295.
5. Tetrahedron Lett. 2010, 51, 6918–6920.
6. J. Am. Chem. Soc. 2011, 133, 15797–15799.
7. Tetrahedron Lett. 1969, 10, 4233–4236. (same paper as Ch. 21 ref 5)
8. Tetrahedron Lett. 2010, 51, 5291–5293.
9. J. Am. Chem. Soc. 2012, 134, 920–923.
10. Org. Lett. 2012, 14, 4738–4741.
11. Can. J. Chem. 2004, 82, 293–300.

### Literature problems

#### 22.110 — book p. 1065 (PDF p. 29) — footnote 1 — J. Am. Chem. Soc. 1954, 76, 2029–2030
- Reaction: cyclohexanone + pyrrolidine, TsOH, −H2O → A; A + 1) CH3I, 2) H3O+ → B + pyrrolidine.
- Asked: structures of A and B (enamine as C-nucleophile; Stork alkylation).
- Expected answer [H]: A = 1-(cyclohex-1-en-1-yl)pyrrolidine `C1CCN(C1)C1=CCCCC1`; C-methylation gives iminium; hydrolysis → B = 2-methylcyclohexanone `CC1CCCCC1=O`.

#### 22.111 — book p. 1065 (PDF p. 29) — footnote 2 — J. Org. Chem. 1977, 42, 1663–1664
- Reaction: cyclohexanone + optically pure trans-2,5-dimethylpyrrolidine (one Me hashed, one wedged → C2-symmetric trans isomer) `C[C@@H]1CC[C@@H](C)N1` (= (2R,5R); trans confirmed, absolute config vs. drawing not verified [L]), TsOH, −H2O → enamine.
- Asked: (a) enamine structure and how many isomers can form; (b) after CH3I then H3O+, 2-methylcyclohexanone obtained in 83% ee — explain the enantiomeric excess (vs racemate in problem 21.107) and identify the major enantiomer.
- Expected answer: (a) [H] only one enamine: the C2 axis makes both N-substituent orientations (rotamers) and both faces relationships identical — single compound. (b) [M] one methyl group of the C2-symmetric pyrrolidine blocks one face of the enamine β-carbon; CH3I approaches from the opposite face → one configuration at the new stereocentre predominates (chiral auxiliary, asymmetric induction). Specific R/S of major product [L] — depends on amine absolute configuration; not determined here (check paper).

#### 22.112 — book p. 1065 (PDF p. 29) — footnote 3 — J. Org. Chem. 2012, 77, 6371–6376
- Context: synthesis of 1,3,6-substituted fulvenes.
- SM: 1-phenylpentane-1,4-dione `CC(=O)CCC(=O)c1ccccc1`. 1) NaOH/H2O, heat; 2) PhMgCl, THF; 3) conc. H2SO4 → C17H14.
- Expected answer [H]: intramolecular aldol condensation (methyl-ketone enolate attacks benzoyl C=O, 5-ring, dehydration) → 3-phenylcyclopent-2-en-1-one `O=C1CCC(c2ccccc2)=C1`; PhMgCl 1,2-addition → 1,3-diphenylcyclopent-2-en-1-ol; acid dehydration → 1,3-diphenylcyclopenta-1,3-diene (C17H14) `C1(c2ccccc2)=CC(c2ccccc2)=CC1` (fulvene precursor).

#### 22.113 — book pp. 1065–1066 (PDF pp. 29–30) — footnote 4 — J. Am. Chem. Soc. 2012, 134, 9291–9295
- Context: synthesis of polycyclic alkaloid **haouamine B**.
- SM: N-R 2,3-dihydropyridin-4(1H)-one-type enone (1,2,3,6-tetrahydropyridin-... ketone) bearing 3-methoxyphenyl on the β-carbon and a 3,5-dimethoxybenzyl group on the adjacent sp3 C–H stereocentre (next to N). Reagent: Tf2O, pyridine. Product: tricyclic indane-fused (hexahydro-indeno[1,2-b]pyridine) with new all-carbon quaternary centre (bearing 3-MeOC6H4) and a vinyl triflate (enol triflate, O–SO2CF3).
- Asked: mechanism; explain stereochemistry of the new chirality centre. (Tf2O activates the Michael acceptor.)
- Expected answer [M-H]: carbonyl O attacks Tf2O → O-triflyl oxocarbenium/allyl cation (very electrophilic β-carbon); the electron-rich dimethoxyaryl ring attacks the β-carbon intramolecularly (Friedel–Crafts-type conjugate addition, 5-membered ring) → arenium ion; pyridine removes H+ to restore aromaticity; product is the enol triflate. Because the tether is short (5-ring), the aryl must add on the same face as the benzyl CH2 → cis ring fusion (new quaternary centre cis to ring-fusion H), i.e. relative stereo set by the existing stereocentre.

#### 22.114 — book p. 1066 (PDF p. 30) — footnote 5 — Tetrahedron Lett. 2010, 51, 6918–6920
- SM 1: α-oxoketene dithioacetal: (E)-4-(p-tolyl)-but-3-enoyl unit attached to C(=C(SEt)2)–C(=O)NH(4-ClC6H4); i.e. ArCH=CH–C(=O)–C(=C(SEt)2)–CONHAr'. + diethyl malonate, K2CO3 → 2: cyclohexenone: C(CO2Et)2 quaternary, C(SEt)=C(CONHAr')–C(=O)–CH2–CH(p-tolyl).
- Expected answer [H]: carbonate deprotonates malonate; malonate enolate adds conjugately to the ketene-dithioacetal carbon, then β-elimination of EtS⁻ (addition–elimination, vinylic substitution); the new malonyl C–H is deprotonated; the carbanion does an intramolecular Michael (6-endo) addition onto the styryl enone β-carbon (CH–Ar); enolate protonation → 2.

#### 22.115 — book p. 1066 (PDF p. 30) — footnote 6 — J. Am. Chem. Soc. 2011, 133, 15797–15799
- Context: total synthesis of **(−)-N-methylwelwitindolinone C isothiocyanate** (blue-green algae; drug-resistant tumours). Key intermediate X (R = protecting group) made from Y with excess NaNH2.
- Y: 3-substituted cyclohexanone (quaternary C bearing Me and vinyl; adjacent C–OR) linked via C(CH3)2 to C3 of 5-bromo-N-methylindole (Br at C5). X: bridged bicyclo[4.3.1] ketone — the cyclohexanone α-carbon bonded to indole C4.
- Asked: (a) mechanism Y → X under strong base; (b) by-product = structural isomer of X lacking a ketone (hint: enolates are ambident).
- Expected answer [H]: NaNH2 forms the ketone enolate and also deprotonates C4–H ortho to Br → elimination of Br⁻ → 4,5-indolyne (aryne); enolate α-carbon attacks the proximal aryne carbon C4 (intramolecular), aryl anion at C5 protonated → X. (b) O-attack of the enolate on the aryne → cyclic aryl enol ether (O-arylation product; C=C–O–Ar, no C=O).

#### 22.116 — book p. 1066 (PDF p. 30) — footnote 7 — Tetrahedron Lett. 1969, 10, 4233–4236
- Reaction: mixture of two conformational (rotameric) enamines of cyclohexanone with ethyl prolinate (pyrrolidine-2-CO2Et, single enantiomer; drawn as CO2Et hashed vs wedged in two orientations) + 1) methyl acrylate, 2) H3O+ → A, methyl 3-(2-oxocyclohexyl)propanoate `O=C1CCCCC1CCC(=O)OC`, 21% ee.
- Asked: assuming equal amounts of both rotamers, explain why A is nearly racemic by analysing the Michael mechanism for each; choose which enantiomer (R/S) of A predominates.
- Expected answer [M]: the CO2Et shields one face of the enamine β-carbon; in the two rotamers the C=C sits on opposite sides of the N–C bond, so the shielded face is reversed → each rotamer delivers the opposite enantiomer → near-cancellation (21% ee from a small difference in reactivity/population). Predominant enantiomer [L] — not determinable without the paper; answer from facial analysis.

#### 22.117 — book p. 1066 (PDF p. 30) — footnote 8 — Tetrahedron Lett. 2010, 51, 5291–5293
- Context: enantiospecific synthesis of sex pheromone of the vine mealybug **Pseudococcus viburni**.
- SM A: cyclic hemiacetal (lactol) 3-OR-4,4-dimethyltetrahydrofuran-2-ol (cf. pantolactone-derived). A + Ph3P=CHCO2Et, heat → B; TsCl, py → C; Me2CuLi → D (ethyl 2-methyl-3-OR-4,4-dimethylcyclopentane... ring with CO2Et, Me, OR, gem-Me2); D → E → F (CH2OAc).
- Asked: (a) B and C; (b) how C → D closes the ring; (c) reagents D → F (two steps) and E.
- Expected answer [H]: (a) lactol opens to hydroxy aldehyde; Wittig → B = (E)-EtO2C–CH=CH–CH(OR)–C(CH3)2–CH2OH (R=H analog: `CCOC(=O)/C=C/C(O)C(C)(C)CO`); C = corresponding primary tosylate (CH2OTs). (b) Me2CuLi 1,4-adds methyl to the enoate β-carbon → ester enolate; enolate α-carbon displaces OTs intramolecularly (SN2, 5-exo-tet) → cyclopentane D (tandem conjugate addition–alkylation). (c) 1) LiAlH4 (xs), then H2O → E (primary alcohol, ring-CH2OH); 2) Ac2O (or AcCl), pyridine → acetate F.

#### 22.118 — book p. 1067 (PDF p. 31) — footnote 9 — J. Am. Chem. Soc. 2012, 134, 920–923
- Context: enantioselective synthesis of **fusarisetin A** (soil fungus; anti-cancer/anti-metastatic without cytotoxicity).
- SM: trans-decalin-fused bicyclic ketone; ring carbon α to the ketone bears an amide C(=O)N(CH3)–CH(CH2OH)–CO2Me (N-methyl-serine methyl ester); adjacent carbon bears CH(OH)CH3. NaOMe → pentacyclic product: pyrrolidine-2,4-dione (tetramic acid) spiro-fused, with hemiketal (C–OH) linked to O of the former CH(OH)CH3 (tetrahydrofuran ring).
- Expected answer [H]: MeO⁻ removes the acidic C–H between ketone and amide carbonyl (β-dicarbonyl) → enolate; Dieckmann: enolate attacks serine methyl ester C=O → tetrahedral intermediate → loses MeO⁻ → 5-membered tetramic-acid ring (new ketone); the secondary OH (side chain) attacks this new ketone intramolecularly → hemiketal (5-ring), proton transfers.

#### 22.119 — book p. 1067 (PDF p. 31) — footnote 10 — Org. Lett. 2012, 14, 4738–4741
- SM 1: cis-2-benzoyl-6-vinyl-3,6-dihydro-2H-pyran `O=C(c1ccccc1)C1CC=CC(C=C)O1` (C14H14O2). 1) NaHMDS, 2) H2O → 2: 2-hydroxy-2-phenylcycloocta-4,6-dien-1-one (8-membered ring, ketone adjacent to C(OH)Ph) `O=C1CC=CC=CCC1(O)c1ccccc1` (C14H14O2, isomer).
- Asked: mechanism incl. stereochemistry (hint: two resonance forms of the first intermediate).
- Expected answer [M-H]: NaHMDS deprotonates C2–H (α to benzoyl) → enolate; resonance form with O⁻ shows ring O–C2=C(O⁻)Ph i.e. an allyl vinyl ether with the 6-vinyl group → anion-accelerated [3,3]-sigmatropic (Claisen) rearrangement: new C–C bond between vinyl terminus and C(Ph)(O⁻), C2–O becomes C=O, C6=C forms → ring-expanded cyclooctadienone alkoxide; H2O protonates → 2. Stereo: the cis-2,6 relationship forces a boat-like TS on one face → chirality transfer to the C(OH)Ph centre (single diastereomer/enantiomer).

#### 22.120 — book p. 1067 (PDF p. 31) — footnote 11 — Can. J. Chem. 2004, 82, 293–300
- Context: ¹⁴C-labelled (at C3) **exo-16,17-dihydro-GA5-13-acetate** (modified gibberellin 1; reduces lodging of cereals).
- SM 2: A-ring-seco gibberellin aldehyde: C10 bears O (γ-lactone O) and –CH2CH2–*CHO (¹⁴C aldehyde); lactone C=O attached to C4–H (with CH3); CO2H, 13-OAc, 16-Me. 1) K2CO3, H2O; 2) HCl → 3: A-ring closed, 3-OH (epimeric, wavy) at *C3.
- Asked: detailed step mechanism 2 → 3.
- Expected answer [M-H]: carbonate deprotonates CO2H, then C4–H α to the lactone carbonyl → lactone enolate; intramolecular aldol addition of C4 onto the ¹⁴C-aldehyde → new 6-membered A ring, alkoxide at C3*; protonation by water → 3-OH (both epimers); HCl reprotonates the carboxylate. (Reverse of a retro-aldol.)

## Chapter 23 — 아민 (Amines)
PDF pp. 32–42 = book pp. 1103–1113.

### Key reactions (반응의 복습, book pp. 1103–1104)
- Preparation: RBr + NaCN then xs LiAlH4 → RCH2NH2; RCOOH → SOCl2, xs NH3 → amide → xs LiAlH4 → amine; benzene → HNO3/H2SO4 → nitrobenzene → H2/Pt or Fe/Zn/Sn/SnCl2, H3O+; NaOH → aniline; azide synthesis (NaN3; H2/Pt or LiAlH4); Gabriel (phthalimide, KOH; RBr; H2NNH2); reductive amination (NH3 / RNH2 / R2NH, [H+], NaBH3CN).
- Reactions: acylation (RNH2 + RCOCl → amide + HCl); Hofmann elimination (xs CH3I; Ag2O, H2O, heat → less-substituted alkene); nitrous acid: 1° amine + NaNO2/HCl → diazonium; 2° → N-nitrosamine.
- Aryl diazonium: Sandmeyer (CuBr, CuCl, CuCN; also CuI), Schiemann (HBF4 → ArF), H2O/heat → phenol, H3PO2 → ArH, azo coupling with activated arenes.
- Heterocycles: pyrrole + Br2, 0 °C → 2-bromopyrrole; pyridine + Br2, 300 °C → 3-bromopyridine.
Summary concepts: amine basicity (pKa of ammonium ions; aryl amines less basic; amides non-basic; pyridine > pyrrole basicity), solvent extraction, 1/2/3° amine IR (two/one N–H bands 3350–3500), ¹H/¹³C shifts.

### Literature problems

#### 23.89 — book p. 1111 (PDF p. 40) — footnote 1 — Tetrahedron Lett. 2012, 53, 1119–1121
- Context: first asymmetric synthesis of alkaloid **(−)-(S,S)-homaline**.
- SM 1: methyl (S)-3-[(3-chloropropyl)amino]-3-phenylpropanoate (2° amine). Product 2: methyl (S)-3-[(3-azidopropyl)(methyl)amino]-3-phenylpropanoate.
- Asked: reagents for the 2-step conversion.
- Expected answer [H]: (1) N-methylation by reductive amination: HCHO (CH2O), [H+], NaBH3CN (avoids over-alkylation to ammonium that CH3I would cause); (2) NaN3 (SN2 on the primary chloride; DMF). (Order interchangeable.)

#### 23.90 — book pp. 1111–1112 (PDF pp. 40–41) — footnote 2 — Bioorg. Med. Chem. Lett. 2006, 16, 2013–2016
- Context: nicotine-like analgesic derivatives with lower toxicity.
- SM 1: 6-chloronicotinic acid `O=C(O)c1ccc(Cl)nc1`. Product 2: N-[(6-chloropyridin-3-yl)methyl]cyclopentanamine `Clc1ccc(CNC2CCCC2)cn1`.
- Expected answer [H]: (1) SOCl2 → acid chloride; (2) cyclopentylamine (2 equiv, or + pyridine) → amide; (3) xs LiAlH4, then H2O → 2° amine. (Alternative: reduce acid to alcohol, oxidise to aldehyde, reductive amination with cyclopentylamine/NaBH3CN; or 6-chloronicotinaldehyde + cyclopentanamine.)

#### 23.91 — book p. 1112 (PDF p. 41) — footnote 3 — Org. Lett. 2007, 9, 2673–2676
- Context: **halosaline** (from Lobelia inflata); synthesis via azide reduction then amide reduction.
- SM: 8-membered lactone with azide: 8-propyl-6-azido-oxocan-2-one (stereo as drawn) `CCC[C@H]1C[C@@H](N=[N+]=[N-])CCCC(=O)O1` (stereo indicative). H2, Pd → 6-(2-hydroxypentyl)piperidin-2-one `CCCC(O)CC1CCCC(=O)N1`; then xs LiAlH4; H2O → halosaline `CCCC(O)CC1CCCCN1` (1-(piperidin-2-yl)pentan-2-ol).
- Asked: show the ring-contraction (lactone → lactam) step.
- Expected answer [H]: H2/Pd reduces N3 → NH2 (loses N2); the amine attacks the lactone C=O intramolecularly (6-membered TS/ring) → tetrahedral intermediate → C–O(ring) bond breaks, releasing the secondary alkoxide → proton transfer → δ-lactam with pendant secondary OH (transamidation; stereocentres retained).

#### 23.92 — book p. 1112 (PDF p. 41) — footnote 4 — J. Am. Chem. Soc. 1945, 67, 860–874
- Context: Woodward–Doering formal total synthesis of **quinine** (1944).
- SM: N-acetyl piperidine with cis-3-(1-aminoethyl) and 4-(CH2CH2CO2Et) substituents `CC(=O)N1CCC(CCC(=O)OCC)C(C(C)N)C1`. 1) xs CH3I; 2) 60% NaOH (heat) → C10H17NO2.
- Expected answer [H]: exhaustive methylation of the 1° amine → –CH(CH3)NMe3⁺; Hofmann elimination (E2, less-substituted alkene) → 3-vinyl; NaOH also hydrolyses the ethyl ester (→ acid) and the N-acetyl amide (→ free NH) → **homomeroquinene**: 3-(3-vinylpiperidin-4-yl)propanoic acid `OC(=O)CCC1CCNCC1C=C` (C10H17NO2 ✓), cis-3,4.

#### 23.93 — book p. 1112 (PDF p. 41) — footnote 5 — Tetrahedron Lett. 2009, 50, 3817–3819
- Context: piperazinone derivatives (migraine, hypertension, etc.).
- SM 1: 2-bromoacetophenone (phenacyl bromide). + L-alanine methyl ester, NaHCO3, DMF → 2 (SN2) `COC(=O)[C@H](C)NCC(=O)c1ccccc1`; + PhCH2NH2, NaBH3CN, AcOH, heat → [3] → 4 (cyclic, lactamization).
- Asked: (a) structure of 3; why diastereomeric mixture; (b) structure of 4; is it a diastereomer mixture? why.
- Expected answer [H]: (a) 3 = methyl N-[2-(benzylamino)-2-phenylethyl]-L-alaninate `COC(=O)[C@H](C)NCC(NCc1ccccc1)c1ccccc1`; reductive amination creates a new stereocentre (CHPh) with no facial control (remote alanine centre) → ~1:1 diastereomers. (b) 4 = 1-benzyl-3-methyl-6-phenylpiperazin-2-one `C[C@@H]1NCC(c2ccccc2)N(Cc2ccccc2)C1=O` (C18H20N2O); yes, still a diastereomeric (cis/trans) mixture because lactamization does not touch either stereocentre (each diastereomer of 3 cyclises separately).

#### 23.94 — book p. 1112 (PDF p. 41) — footnote 6 — Tetrahedron Lett. 2004, 45, 5807–5810
- Context: designed anticancer alkylating agent (SAM/adenosine-based).
- SM: 5'-amino-5'-deoxy-2',3'-bis-O-(triethylsilyl)adenosine (C22H42N6O3Si2) + ethyl (E)-4,5-dibromopent-2-enoate (EtO2C–CH=CH–CHBr–CH2Br), DBU → C29H50N6O5Si2.
- Asked: structure of product (two sequential substitutions).
- Expected answer [H]: N-substituted **aziridine**: amine displaces one bromide (SN2), then the resulting 2° amine closes intramolecularly on the vicinal bromide → 2-[(E)-2-(ethoxycarbonyl)vinyl]aziridine attached at N to the 5'-CH2 of adenosine. SMILES: `Nc1ncnc2c1ncn2[C@@H]1O[C@H](CN3CC3/C=C/C(=O)OCC)[C@@H](O[Si](CC)(CC)CC)[C@H]1O[Si](CC)(CC)CC` — formula C29H50N6O5Si2 ✓ (RDKit). Aziridine = alkylating group.

#### 23.95 — book pp. 1112–1113 (PDF pp. 41–42) — footnote 7 — Tetrahedron Lett. 2005, 46, 979–982
- Context: enaminoesters (vinylogous carbamates) as N-heterocycle building blocks; one-pot.
- SM 1: tert-butyl (2-azidopropyl)carbamate (Boc-NH-CH2-CH(CH3)-N3, stereocentre). H2, Pd → [2]; with methyl acetoacetate present, 2 → imine [3] without acid; 3 tautomerises → enamine 4: methyl (Z)-3-[(1-(Boc-amino)propan-2-yl)amino]but-2-enoate `COC(=O)/C=C(/C)NC(C)CNC(=O)OC(C)(C)C`.
- Asked: (a) 2 and 3; (b) acid-catalysed (H3O+) mechanism 3 → 4; (c) at least two reasons equilibrium favours enamine 4 over imine 3.
- Expected answer [H]: (a) 2 = tert-butyl (2-aminopropyl)carbamate `CC(N)CNC(=O)OC(C)(C)C`; 3 = ketimine: MeO2C–CH2–C(CH3)=N–CH(CH3)CH2NHBoc. (b) protonate imine N → iminium; water/base removes α-C–H (CH2 between C=N and ester) → enamine; (c) enamine C=C conjugated with ester C=O (push–pull/vinylogous carbamate resonance) and a 6-membered intramolecular N–H···O=C hydrogen bond (Z-isomer); also α-H is acidic (β-dicarbonyl-like).

#### 23.96 — book p. 1113 (PDF p. 42) — footnote 8 — J. Org. Chem. 2011, 76, 1605–1613
- Context: total synthesis of **crispine A** (cytotoxic alkaloid, Mongolian thistle).
- SM 1: methyl (S)-2-amino-3-(3,4-dimethoxyphenyl)propanoate `COC(=O)[C@@H](N)Cc1ccc(OC)c(OC)c1`; + 4-chlorobutanal, [H3O+] → 2: methyl 8,9-dimethoxy-1,2,3,5,6,10b-hexahydropyrrolo[2,1-a]isoquinoline-5-carboxylate `COc1cc2c(cc1OC)C1CCCN1[C@H](C(=O)OC)C2` (C16H21NO4).
- Expected answer [M-H]: imine formation (carbinolamine, −H2O) → iminium; Pictet–Spengler: electron-rich aryl ring (para to OMe) attacks iminium carbon (6-endo) → arenium, loss of H⁺ → tetrahydroisoquinoline (2° amine); intramolecular N-alkylation (SN2 on CH2Cl) closes the pyrrolidine ring → 2. (Alternative order: N-alkylation first → cyclic pyrrolinium ion → Pictet–Spengler.)

#### 23.97 — book p. 1113 (PDF p. 42) — footnote 9 — J. Org. Chem. 1999, 64, 4966–4968
- Context: ring-opened analogue of **epibatidine** (potent analgesic, Ecuadorian poison frog); fragmentation reaction.
- SM: N-ethoxycarbonyl-8-azabicyclo[3.2.1]octan-3-ol bearing 3-(6-chloropyridin-3-yl) (tertiary alcohol) `CCOC(=O)N1C2CCC1CC(O)(c1ccc(Cl)nc1)C2` [M on skeleton]; CF3CO2H, NaBH3CN → N-CO2Et-2-[2-(6-chloropyridin-3-yl)allyl]pyrrolidine `CCOC(=O)N1CCCC1CC(=C)c1ccc(Cl)nc1`.
- Expected answer [M-H]: TFA protonates OH, loss of H2O → tertiary (benzylic-type) carbocation at C3; Grob-type fragmentation: N lone pair pushes, C1–C2 bond cleaves → C2=C3 (1,1-disubstituted alkene) + N-acyliminium at C1; NaBH3CN delivers hydride to the iminium → pyrrolidine.

#### 23.98 — book p. 1113 (PDF p. 42) — footnote 10 — J. Org. Chem. 1999, 64, 4617–4626
- SM: 2-aminofuran tethered N(R)–CH2CH2–C(=CH2)CO2Me (2-[N-R-amino]furan with methyl 2-methylenebutanoate chain). Heat, MeOH → hexahydroindole: N-R, C7a=C7 enamine, C3a quaternary CO2Me, C6-OMe and C5-OH (trans, MeO hashed / OH wedged as drawn).
- Asked: mechanism and the stereochemistry of the product.
- Expected answer [M]: intramolecular Diels–Alder of the furan (diene) with the acrylate (dienophile) (IMDAF) → oxa-bridged (7-oxabicyclo[2.2.1]heptene) cycloadduct fused to a pyrrolidine; N lone pair drives C7a–O bridge cleavage → conjugated iminium + C5-alkoxide (→ C5-OH, stereo fixed by the former bridge, cis to the CO2Me-bearing ring fusion face); MeOH adds conjugately (1,4) to the α,β-unsaturated iminium at C6 from the face opposite the OH → enamine (C7=C7a); relative stereo: OH and OMe trans; CO2Me configuration set by the cycloaddition.

## Notes / caveats
- All 42 footnoted problems across ch. 20–23 are listed (ch. 20: footnotes 2–12, footnote 1 not in these pages; ch. 21: 1–10; ch. 22: 1–11; ch. 23: 1–10).
- Citations are transcribed exactly as printed; Ch. 20 ref 4 shows volume "43" for Tetrahedron 2014 (likely misprint for 70).
- Ch. 21 ref 5 and Ch. 22 ref 7 are the same paper (Tetrahedron Lett. 1969, 10, 4233) — both about proline-ester enamines of cyclohexanone.
- Lower-confidence items: 20.88 (Claisen route assumed), 21.88 (quadrone rearrangement details), 22.111/22.116 absolute configurations, 23.98 stereo rationale.
